  - The steady bound stays below maxQuoteAge.
  - One transition per facts-stop may reach 2 × release + stale + every + latency (2.7 s). An exit then waits for a fresh market (EXIT-1d) and is never priced stale.

"No rent recovery" is a reported sensitivity line. Worst-case reservations keep full rent. No statistical threshold changes.

The Holm family stays at 2 (U1, U2). BT-2 refuses a freeze if the pre-registration hash differs. A later variant is a 7th trial and needs a new holdout window.

The full 19 Jul – 3 Oct pull projects to about 18.95M credits (about US$94 on Developer; 5–6 days at 40 blocks/s is unmeasured and could take 1–3 weeks).

Supervisor rulings:
- **One rent model in the proof's scoring.** RENT-1 (#114) is the only rent model: per candidate, core's `accountGetsDust` and `closeSucceeds` decide whether rent comes back (the close lands with no dust) or a failed close pays one failed exit attempt. RES-4's cost math reads the same terms (P(back) = (1 − dust) × close, P(failed close) = (1 − dust)(1 − close)) and adds no second model to `outcome.ts`. RES-4's `scoringTerms()` export is allowed only as a pure refactor, proven by a golden test that the outcome stage scores byte-identically.
- **Proof-scoring changes get their own PR.** The slot-counted observation tail (from RES-3b) changes how outcomes are scored, so it leaves the research PRs (#56, #120) for BT-TAIL, with a test that fails before and passes after and the BT reviewer.
- **"Entries: On" only when it is true (API-1).** The app's status card may say entries are on only when no halt, no risk stop (daily, weekly or session loss reached, the kill latch, the loss pauses) and no stale regime evaluation stands. The worker serves the risk stops read-only as halt codes; if that needs a change under `packages/core/src/risk/**`, the card keeps APP-3's rule and never says "On". A notRunning reply is refused for paper, so it can never mask a paper outage.
- **The practice-day pull fails closed on credits (#119).** A credit booking that fails stops the day (exit 1, not resumable), the usage file is written atomically, and `rpc_rps` is bounded 1–50 (5 on the free plan).
- **The live worker owns exits, so it is the last process the kernel kills (SWITCH-1).** `zeroed-worker.service` runs at OOMScoreAdjust −500 under its MemoryMax (its own cgroup kills first); the switch's confined trial runs at +1000 and never copies the worker's score. SIGN-1's signer unit gets −500 when it lands.

Deploy was held. #126 fixed the wait (this invocation, then /health with that boot). After any ops merge, the push e2e must be green before Deploy, and that is checked by hand until OPS-GATE merges.

## Order and position lifecycle (CORE-1, `packages/core/src/lifecycle`)

- **2026-10-03 · A failed signature read is terminal only at `finalized`.** A failure read at `processed` or `confirmed` may come from a fork that is later dropped, and the original transaction could still land. Acting on it would allow a replacement, which could mean a second buy or an oversell. Waiting for `finalized` costs about 13 s. A success read counts from `confirmed`: booking a fill early is safe, because the books stay open until every other attempt is dead.
- **2026-10-03 · An attempt is dead only when it has failed at `finalized`, or when the confirmed block height has passed its `lastValidBlockHeight`.** A replacement may be signed, and the books closed, only when every other attempt is dead. Each of these events carries the block height it was read at: `sign_replacement` and `reconcile`.
- **2026-10-03 · Balances are the truth.** A fill found after a failed or expired status is booked with a critical alert. If more than one attempt landed, all are booked (`double_fill` alert). An exit that sold more than its quantity is booked, not refused (`oversold` alert).
- **2026-10-03 · A landing after an intent ended is never refused or left to a person.** This replaces an earlier choice made the same day, which the review rejected. It applies to a landing reported for a cancelled, abandoned or filled intent.
  - The landing raises `unbooked_landing` and a `reconcile_orphan` effect. Every tick repeats the effect until the wallet reconciliation books it with the book's `orphan_fill`.
  - A late buy gets its own open position, so exits can protect it at once. A late sell reduces the position it sold from.
  - New entries stay blocked until it is booked; exits are never blocked.
- **2026-10-03 · A refused reconcile is retried.** While an outcome is known but not reconciled, every tick asks for `reconcile_balances` again, so a reconcile refused because another attempt could still land is retried until it succeeds.
- **2026-10-03 · A late landing reported on a dropped fork is cleared only with finalized proof.** The book's `orphan_cleared` event lifts the entry hold, with an alert, only when all of these hold:
  - balances are unchanged at `finalized`;
  - a `finalized` status read for the signature either failed, or, with a history search, found nothing once the *finalized* block height is past the attempt's last valid height.
  The finalized height matters, because a landing just before expiry is not finalized yet. Without this proof the hold stays, which fails safe. Without the event at all, a fork-dropped report would block entries forever and stop unattended paper runs.
- **2026-10-03 · A restart while `signed` never sends the bytes.** The intent becomes `unknown` and waits for expiry, because we cannot prove whether the bytes left before the restart.

## Engine (ENG-1, `packages/core/src/engine`)

- **2026-10-03 · The engine refuses events outside the total order; a live Feed must release in that order.** Events are ordered by (slot, transaction index, instruction index, receipt time, id). An event that is not after the previous one is logged `out_of_order` and refused, and one dated after now is logged `future_event`. Live, facts can arrive late (a slot-99 transaction after a slot-100 one, or after an off-chain status read in its own slot). So the live Feed holds facts behind a slot horizon and releases them in total order. The parity test replays the *recorded release sequence*, refused events included, rather than re-sorting the raw facts. Re-sorting would deliver facts that live dropped, and the decision logs would differ.
- **2026-10-03 · Repeated reconciles are de-duplicated and rate-limited, and no key waits without bound.** The same `reconcile_balances` or `reconcile_orphan` is sent at most once per `minSlotsBetween`, and at most `maxPerWindow` reconciles go out per `windowSlots`. Under that cap:
  - Orphans (unbooked landings, which block every entry) go before balance reads.
  - Each window reserves max(1, ⌊cap/4⌋) places for waiting balance reads, never more than cap − 1, so exits still reconcile while orphan reads keep failing. With a cap of 1, the two kinds alternate window by window.
  - Within a kind, the key served least recently goes first. A key never served counts as served at its first ask, so a key asked continuously waits at most ⌈K / share⌉ + 1 windows.
  - A waiter that was not asked for in the last slot leaves the line but keeps its turn.

  Earlier versions served first come, first served (which starved orphans), then in rounds by send count (which let returning keys starve a persistent one). This version changes which key is sent compared with those, by design: it is a fairness fix, not a refactor.
- **2026-10-03 · Everything a strategy can reach is frozen.** The book, each decision before it is applied, the effects and the log records are all deep-frozen. A strategy cannot change engine state, or a decision after making it, outside the lifecycle. The log cannot drift from its hash. The replay freezes a copy of its input, never the caller's objects.
- **2026-10-03 · Only the replay driver and its fill model hold a `Replay`.** `momentOf` and `pending` reveal whether future events exist, so neither the engine nor a strategy is given one. The engine gets only the `Clock` and the `Feed`.
- **2026-10-03 · Effect runners are synchronous.** Results come back as feed events. A runner that returns a promise is refused, because results it scheduled after an `await` would arrive after the replay ended and be lost.

## Evidence (`packages/core/src/domain`)

- **2026-10-03 · `checkFreshness` checks age and timestamps only.** It does not reject evidence flagged `fork-suspect`, `provider-degraded`, `partial` or `estimated`, nor evidence read at `processed` commitment. The evidence-gates task must reject these: unknown or degraded evidence is a failure, never a pass. Until that gate exists, `checkFreshness` alone does not prove evidence usable.

## Ledger and storage (LEDGER-1, `packages/core/src/ledger`)

- **2026-10-03 · Outcomes live in a second file.** Labels, the experiment registry and gate results go in a scoring file (`@meme-snipe/core/ledger/scoring`), not in the ledger. The ledger's entry point never imports the scoring module, never attaches a file, keeps its connection out of reach (no property holds it) and refuses to open a scoring file. An import guard test starts from every source file outside `ledger/` and `stats/`, follows static, dynamic, `require`, re-export and package-subpath imports transitively, and fails on any path to the scoring store, a ledger internal or `node:sqlite`. Loads it cannot follow also fail it: a computed `import()` or `require()`, `createRequire`, `process.getBuiltinModule`, native bindings, and any string naming sqlite. Gate results count as outcomes: the worker reads them through the scoring reader, never through the engine.
- **2026-10-03 · Amounts are canonical decimal text.** A u64 token amount can pass SQLite's signed 64-bit integer, so every amount column is `TEXT` with a check (digits only, no leading zero, no sign, no decimal point) and is read back as `bigint`. Label net P&L may be negative (`net_lamports`). Return fractions and probabilities stay `REAL`: they are ratios, not money.
- **2026-10-03 · Append-only by trigger.** Every table refuses `UPDATE` and `DELETE`. Every connection the ledger and scoring code open sets `recursive_triggers = ON`, so `INSERT OR REPLACE` fires the delete guard; upserts fire the update guard. A connection opened outside this code without that pragma could still REPLACE a row: the triggers protect against our own code, not against someone with raw file access. State changes are new rows: intent and position events, a release or keep row per reservation, a done row per outbox item, a result row per operator command.
- **2026-10-03 · One writer by OS lock.** (Replaces a pid lock file, which the review showed two processes could both take.) The writer holds `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` with `locking_mode = EXCLUSIVE` on a sidecar SQLite file `<file>-writer.lock` for its whole life. The kernel drops the lock when the process dies, so there is no stale-lock handling and no race. A second opener in any process is refused after about 1.5 s of retries. Openers racing for a fresh lock can deadlock inside SQLite: each holds a read lock while waiting for the others to drop theirs, and CI caught a run where all six were refused. So each attempt does not wait. On failure it closes, which drops its read lock, then pauses a random 5–45 ms (`crypto.randomInt`, so racers fall out of step) and retries, up to 60 times. One racer wins. The lock lives in `ledger/adapters/lock.ts`, because pausing the thread and drawing randomness are allowed only in `adapters/` folders. A lock file with foreign content fails closed. Reservation atomicity does not rely on this lock: it runs in `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and is tested with eight processes on separate connections.
- **2026-10-03 · Times come from the caller.** The ledger never reads the clock, so a backtest on a simulated clock writes the same rows as live. This also keeps it clean under ENG-1's purity guard (PR #10). `schema_migrations` records version, name and checksum, with no applied time.
- **2026-10-03 · A file is stamped with its purpose** (`live`, `paper` or `backtest`) when created, and only opens as that purpose, so a backtest can never write into a live ledger.
- **2026-10-03 · Migrations are forward-only and checked.** Versions run 1..n with no gaps, each in its own transaction, recorded with a SHA-256 of kind, version, name and SQL. A file newer than the code, or one whose applied migration differs from the code's (an edit, or the other kind of file), is refused. Schema 1 holds frozen copies of the domain's venue and status lists; a test fails when the domain lists change, so the change ships as migration 2.
- **2026-10-03 · Telegram may only pause, and issuers are roles.** The schema refuses any other command with auth level `telegram` (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §12), and `issued_by` must be `owner` or `system`, so no Telegram id or email can be stored.
- **2026-10-03 · Node version.** `node:sqlite` is unflagged from 22.13, and the crash tests run child processes with type stripping (unflagged from 22.18). The root `engines` field is `>=22.18`; CI's `node-version: 22` resolves to 22.22.
- **2026-10-03 · Reservation limits fail closed.** A missing or malformed limit (`maxHeld` not a bigint ≥ 0, `maxCount` not a safe integer ≥ 1) throws and writes nothing, never lets a reservation through.
- **2026-10-03 · The ledger stores intent statuses; it does not judge them.** The CORE-1 reducer is the authority on legal sequences. A status-only check in the ledger would refuse legal ones, such as a late landing after `reconciled`. Accepted by review on condition that the replay check is tracked by name: **LEDGER-REPLAY**, under pre-funding gate item 2. LEDGER-REPLAY replays every stored intent and position event through the CORE-1 reducer and fails on any illegal sequence. To be built with BT-1 (it needs the stored events with their full payloads) and added to the task board by the supervisor.
- **2026-10-03 · Guard rules match ENG-1's.** ENG-1's purity guard (PR #10) forbids I/O, clocks, randomness, dynamic `import`/`require` and global escape hatches in core outside `adapters/` folders, which it skips entirely. It has a named `ledger/` exemption that allows `node:sqlite`, and it checks every string in `ledger/` for SQL that reads the clock or randomness, or that attaches or detaches a file. So the ledger's SQL stays in `ledger/`, where that check runs (an earlier move of it into `ledger/adapters/` was reverted because it took the SQL out of the check). Only the writer lock, which needs `Atomics` and `randomInt`, is in `ledger/adapters/lock.ts`, and it holds no ledger SQL. Since ENG-1 merged, its guard applies that SQL check to every string in `ledger/**`, `adapters/` included, so the ledger keeps no separate SQL test (one was used until then and removed as a duplicate). The ledger's import guard adds the outcome-store rules: no `node:sqlite`, `getBuiltinModule`, `createRequire`, computed import or require, native bindings or sqlite-naming string outside `ledger/` and `stats/`. Checked against the merged guard (base 3f080cf). Limit: no static scan stops code written on purpose to get around it (for example a module name built at run time behind `globalThis[...]`; ENG-1's global-escape-hatch rule catches that particular form). The guards cover every ordinary way to load a module; deliberate evasion is for code review to catch.

### Ledger replay check (LEDGER-REPLAY, `packages/core/src/ledger/replay`, `pnpm ledger:replay`)

- **2026-10-03 · Book events are stored in the existing `detail` column, with no new table (approved by the supervisor, same day).** The ledger had no book-event log, and `intent_event.seq` and `position_event.seq` are separate sequences. `intent_event.seq` is the global order. Each stored book event writes one intent row per intent the reducer persists, in effect order. The first row carries `detail = {"v":1,"book":<BookEvent>}`, and later rows of the same event carry `null`. A `created` row stands for `propose_entry` (entry) or `trigger_exit` (exit), whose reasons are kept in that step's `position_event.detail` in the same versioned form. An event that persists no intent but changes the book or a position writes one row, with the status unchanged, for the intent it concerns: a landing reported for an ended intent, `orphan_cleared`, or a trigger merged into a running exit. `stepRows` decides the rows, so writers and the replay share one rule. BT-1 and the worker must write this way; `test/ledger/recorder.ts` shows how.
- **2026-10-03 · The stored event is versioned and checked strictly (supervisor's condition).** `encodeBookDetail` (on write) and `decodeBookDetail` (on read), in `ledger/replay/schema.ts`, refuse an unknown or missing version, a missing or extra field at any depth, a wrong type, a non-canonical id or signature, and a negative or non-bigint amount. Events the replay rebuilds from the intent and position tables pass the same check. A new field or event type needs version 2.
- **2026-10-03 · Not stored, not checked:** pause and resume, a restart that changes no intent, and ticks. They only refuse entries, so leaving them out can make the replay more permissive but never changes a replayed state. The default open-position limit is "none" for the same reason. `--max-open-positions` checks a session's limit.
- **2026-10-03 · Read-only means the ledger bytes never change.** The replay opens the file with `openLedgerReader` (SQLite read-only). On a WAL file, SQLite still creates its empty `-wal` and its `-shm` lock index beside the file. These hold no ledger data, and the tests check that the `-wal` stays empty and the file is unchanged byte for byte.
- **2026-10-03 · Stamps.** The file's purpose stamp decides which decision modes it may hold: `backtest` holds only `replay`, `paper` holds `paper` and `shadow`, and `live` holds `live` and `shadow` (shadow runs beside paper and live, quant.md §8). The command refuses files with different stamps in one run.
- **2026-10-03 · Compaction.** The CORE-1 book copies its maps on every event, so a replay of the whole book slowed with the square of the ledger's size (1.9k events a second at 2.5k intents). The replay moves ended intents and closed positions out of the book every 64 events. It brings one back before any event that names it (by intent id, idempotency key, position id or orphan signature). The reducer reads the others only to skip them, so states are unchanged. A test compares the compacted and uncompacted reports on honest and tampered ledgers. The engine pays the same quadratic cost during a backtest, which is a risk for BT-1's throughput target. The replay also reads `fill` and `attempt` once each, grouped by intent, because `fill` has no index on `intent_id` and a read per intent scanned the whole table. Measured on engine-generated backtest ledgers: 22.2k events a second at both 21k and 63k events (7.4k intents), so the cost now grows linearly.
- **2026-10-03 · Whole tables, not only known intents (review of 611a4bb).** A fill, attempt, reservation or reservation event for an intent that does not exist would pass a check that reads rows only by known intent ids. The replay therefore also runs `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` (read-only) and requires the `fill`, `attempt`, `reservation` and `reservation_event` totals to equal the replayed totals. Each table has a test that fails on 611a4bb.
- **2026-10-03 · Named allowance: "held while the outbox row is pending".** When the reducer has released or kept a reservation, the stored reservation may still read held while the `release_reservation` or `keep_reservation` outbox row for that intent is not yet done. The runner ends the reservation after the state change commits. Once that outbox row is done, a reservation still held fails the check. Tests cover both cases.
- **2026-10-03 · Not compared by the replay:** the contents of `fee`, `outbox` (beyond the allowance above), `outbox_done`, `decision`, `feature_snapshot`, `observation` and `operator_command`, and every event and row timestamp (`ts`, `created_ts`). The reducer produces none of these, so there is nothing to replay them against. Their stamps and foreign keys are still checked.
- **2026-10-03 · LEDGER-1b: many positions per entry intent (migration 2; supervisor approved the data shape).** A buy that lands after its entry ended gets its own position (`<position>.o<n>`) with the same entry intent, so `position.entry_intent_id` is no longer `UNIQUE`. The position table is rebuilt with the same columns and checks, a plain index on `entry_intent_id`, and its append-only triggers restored. Rows are copied out and back, not renamed, because `ALTER TABLE RENAME` opens SQLite's temp schema on the writer's connection, and the label guard requires that connection to hold only `main`. A migration that rebuilds tables sets `rebuildsTables`. The runner then switches foreign keys off before `BEGIN` (SQLite's 12-step ALTER) and runs `foreign_key_check` before commit, rolling back on any orphan. It switches foreign keys back on whatever happens. The flag is not part of the checksum, so migration 1's checksum is unchanged. Before this fix, a late buy could not be stored and the replay failed it with table `position`. It is now stored and replays.
- **2026-10-03 · LEDGER-1c: reservations carry the account version of their risk snapshot.** `reserveExposure` refuses `stale_snapshot` unless the account version read inside the same BEGIN IMMEDIATE equals the version the decision saw (`accountVersion()`); so two entries decided from one snapshot can never both be stored, even after the first one's reservation ended in a position (risk review of PR #18, item 1). The version is derived, not stored: the sum of the row counts of the append-only tables that change a risk snapshot (reservation, reservation_event, fill, fee, position, position_event, operator_command, command_result), so it only rises and cannot drift; no migration is needed. Intents, intent events and attempts are left out (an entry writes them itself between its decision and its reservation; an unresolved entry is already counted by its reservation). The check fails closed: in a live or paper ledger a reservation, or a book event that creates one, without a version throws; only a backtest ledger, which mirrors decisions already made, may omit it. `recordBookEvent` checks the version once, before the event writes anything. A risk snapshot is built with `withSnapshot(read)` (reader and writer): the version and every inner read share one read transaction, so a write landing meanwhile cannot change what the snapshot saw (risk review of PR #40). Every ledger table is classified: counted (`ACCOUNT_VERSION_TABLES`) or excluded with its reason (`ACCOUNT_VERSION_EXCLUDED`); a test fails on any unclassified table, so a new one (owner latches, deposits and withdrawals) cannot be missed. The data shape is approved under the supervisor ruling in `CLAUDE.md` (the bot's own state, no personal data).
- **2026-10-03 · A mark update cannot change an allowed reservation, so marks are not in the version.** An entry is allowed only with open positions + unresolved entries < maxOpen, and maxOpen ≤ 1 in every approved baseline (tighten-only), so an allowed entry's snapshot has no open position; marks enter the R6 figures only through open positions, so they contribute nothing to its limit. A position that opens after the snapshot advances the version (position, position_event). If the owner ever approves maxOpen > 1, marks must join the version (or the limit be re-read in the reservation) before that baseline is used.

## Configuration and policy (CFG-1, `packages/core/src/config`)

- **2026-10-03 · Code loads limits and tightens them; it never raises them.** A session starts only from a policy that is tighter than or equal to an approved baseline on every field. Baselines are a frozen list in `baselines.ts`, starting with the trial preset. Raising a limit means adding a baseline in a reviewed pull request (the owner's step); nothing at runtime can add one.
- **2026-10-03 · Every field has a direction.** `max` (a cap or trigger: may fall only), `min` (a floor: may rise only), `locked` (any change needs a new version), `free` (the label). A new field without a direction does not compile.
- **2026-10-03 · The exit path is locked.** Exit fees, the fee ceiling, min-out and the emergency rung cannot be changed by override, and validation refuses a zero fee or a zero slippage allowance on any rung. A lower number there can stop an exit landing, so it is never "tighter".
- **2026-10-03 · A saved policy is read by a strict reader.** Duplicate keys and `__proto__`, `constructor` and `prototype` keys are refused; every field is checked and covered by the hash; a field supplied through a prototype is not a field. The session copies, then validates, then locks the copy.
- **2026-10-03 · Values deferred to the cards that own them** (new policy schema version when they land): the H15 round-trip tolerance, the regime's execution-health thresholds and entry slippage (GATE-1 and RISK-1). The H13 dev-cluster cap (5%) and the H14 14-day rug look-back are in the policy now.
- **2026-10-03 · The money-literal scan reads tokens.** It knows regex literals and decodes string escapes. It flags any number of 1,000 or more in any notation, any nonzero literal in a statement with `MICRO_PER_USD` or `LAMPORTS_PER_SOL` or inside a money constructor call, numeric strings given to `BigInt`, `Number`, `parseInt` and `parseFloat`, and money constructors built from a literal, anywhere in `packages/core/src` outside `config/`. The allow-list is by exact file, declared name and value, and a test fails if an entry goes stale. Limit: an amount assembled from small numbers is not seen.
- **2026-10-04 · The host runs the release's own worker (SWITCH-1).** `ops/host-config.json` now says `"worker": "release"`; the supervisor merges it only after WORKER-1b #82, WORKER-1c #99 and RISK-MARK #103.
  - Before `zeroed-update` changes anything for a new release (host files, `current`, the restart), it runs `worker-smoke`: a trial start of that release's worker beside the running one.
    - The trial runs as transient systemd units under zeroed-worker.service's own sandbox, limits and environment file, read from the installed unit (`unit_sandbox`). It has no credentials, a scratch state directory as its only writable path, its own loopback ports (8797 and 8798), and memory capped at 280M (review #110: the host has 1 GB and the live worker keeps running).
      - **2026-10-07 · Note (Z00):** the host is now the 2 GB `vc2-1c-2gb` (D07). The 280M cap, `MemoryMax=800M` and `--max-old-space-size=560` stay as they are: only the stand-in runs, and the Blueprint recorder card (Z10) sizes its own unit from measurement.
    - `--reconcile` must exit 0. Health must then answer in paper within 90 s, and the trial must still run and answer 30 s later.
  - A failed trial leaves everything as it is, sends one alert and retries every 5 minutes. A release with a worker that cannot start therefore never stops the one that runs.
  - After the switch, the new worker must answer in paper within 60 s, with /health's `git_sha` equal to the new commit (OPS-1i: an answer from the worker that ran before never counts), and then run 30 s with no restart, still answering as that commit. If it does not, current, the deployed record, the host files (the previous release's `install.sh --update`) and the worker go back to the release that ran. The commit is recorded in `failed_release` and not tried again (a newer deploy is), and one alert is sent. This covers a worker that passes the trial but fails under the unit, for example only once it has its credentials.
  - The trial uses no credentials on purpose: provider keys are not needed to start (without providers the worker runs degraded, entries halted), and the trial must not touch the unit's credentials or state.
  - The e2e proves the release's worker on the host image: Node 22 type stripping with no node_modules, reconcile first, keys from systemd credentials and absent from its output, loopback health and API, degraded without providers (the test server reaches none), clean restart, live refused. It also proves a green signed release with a broken worker stays undeployed.
  - Provider URLs cannot be pointed at the fake services without a worker change, so "unreachable providers" is the tested case.
  - The worker API does not serve the README contract's `GET /health` `evidence` list (the stand-in did). `zeroed-status` still counts the evidence from the index; serving it from the API is left to an API card.
- **2026-10-04 · Worker card (APP-3).** The app cannot reach the worker's `/health`: it listens on loopback (8787) and the app reads only the read API (8788), whose `/api/v1/paper/status` serves `connected`, `flags` and `risk`. So the dashboard's Worker section shows a card built from those flags only. Entries shows `Off: paused`, `stale data`, `regime` or `no evidence`. Candidates shows `None yet`. Exits shows `Blocked` or `Pending`. Alerts lists unknown transaction result, low fee reserve and rate limited. A row appears only when a flag proves it; unknown flags add nothing; a disconnected worker shows only "Worker not connected". The card never says entries are on or exits are ready, because the status endpoint does not carry what would prove it. Fields for the worker-API card to add to `status`, from what `/health` already holds: `haltReasons` (every reason entries are off: seeding, divergence, budget halt, the feed and pause reasons), `exitCapable`, `alerts` (critical alerts), and `regime` with its state and reason (for example, volume days known out of the window). The card shows each one once it is served and validated by the app's strict schema.
- **2026-10-04 · Worker status fields (API-1).** `/api/v1/paper/status` adds four fields. They are read from state the worker already holds; the API stays GET-only on loopback, and nothing new is written to disk.
  - `haltReasons`: each entry-halt reason as a code, with `source` naming the feed or provider. The codes are starting, feed-stale, feed-disconnected, feed-dropped, paused, seeding, divergence, and budget (the scheduler's quota halt). Text the API does not know is `other`.
  - `exitCapable`: `/health`'s exit_capable.
  - `alerts`: the engine's critical alerts since boot. Each code and subject is kept once, at its first sighting, and only the newest 50 are kept, in memory only.
  - `regime`: the latest regime evaluation (state, time, and each reason's code and input). The strategy evaluates the regime first for every candidate, so the latest candidate's evaluation is the current state as of its time. It is null before the first candidate.
  - "Not running" (supervisor, from real use: the app showed "Server error" for 10 minutes after the owner visited Live or Backtest, because a paper-only worker answered those modes with 404). Every app path of a mode the worker does not run (`live`, `backtest`) now answers 200 with `{mode, asOf, data: null, notRunning}`. The app accepts exactly that shape: the connection counts it as a good answer, and sections show "Not running". Unknown modes and paths stay 404.
  - The worker API has no `/health` and does not serve the dry-run evidence (only the host's stand-in did). ARCHITECTURE §12.4 and ops/README now say so: the evidence stays in the host index, which `zeroed-status` reads. Adding it to the API would be a new served data surface for no current reader.

  In the app schema the four fields are optional and strict: a worker without them still validates, and a malformed one is refused whole. The card shows a row only when a field proves it:
  - Entries shows `On` only with no halt reason, the regime on and no stopping flag. A halt reason the app does not name still reads `Off`.
  - Regime shows `On` or `Off: <reasons>`; an unknown input reads `<input> unknown`.
  - Exits shows `Ready` or `Not ready`; a blocked or pending exit flag takes precedence.
  - Alerts are listed by name.
  - Unknown values add nothing.

  Review fixes (01DdN4's FAIL at 7433148, supervisor ruling):
  - Risk stops in status. The card showed "Entries: On" while a risk stop refused every entry, because daily, weekly and kill-switch loss and the loss pauses live only in the candidate path. The strategy now reads core risk's tripped entry controls (`evaluateExit`'s `tripped`, the same inputs as an exit; read only, nothing latched) on the account's own event and at least once per second of event time. Status serves each as a halt code (daily-loss, weekly-loss, weekly-review, kill-switch, wallet-below-kill-line, loss-cooldown, loss-day-pause, loss-review, session-ended, max-open-positions; any other is `risk` with the core code as source). `risk-unknown` is served when the account cannot be read, before the first event, or when the reading is over 5 s old. The status's own daily-loss meter at its limit also serves daily-loss. No change under `packages/core/src/risk/**`.
  - Status judges the account as the entry path does (01DdN4's second FAIL at ca0648c): marked with no fallback, so a mark that fails reads `risk-unknown` (the entry refuses with risk-mark-failed); and `riskSnapshot` first, which is null exactly when core's account check throws (where `evaluateExit` would report nothing tripped), so that also reads `risk-unknown`. Worker only. A fault code from `evaluateExit` itself is a separate core card (RISK-FAULT).
  - Regime freshness. "Entries: On" and "Regime: On" need a regime evaluation at most `regimeMaxAgeMs` old: two candidate evaluation steps (`evaluateEveryMs`, which is the policy's `maxQuoteAgeMs`, 2 s on the trial policy, settings.ts). The worker evaluates the regime first for every candidate at that step, so an older evaluation means no candidate is being judged. Status serves `regime.current`; when it is false the card shows "Regime: Not checked lately".
  - S0 diagnostic (supervisor ruling, after WORKER-1e): status serves `regime.waived`, the regime parts the diagnostic set did not judge. With any of them the card reads "Regime: On (practice: <parts> not judged)" and "Entries: On (practice)", never a plain "On"; a regime with no waived list served shows neither.
  - A paper answer with `notRunning` is bad data (`bad-shape`), never "Not running": every server runs paper.
  - 2026-10-05 · `regime.waived` under the set is the whole set (N1', re-rated blocking by the run/CI reviewer). While `ZEROED_S0_DIAGNOSTIC` is on, status serves every part the set configures (`S0_DIAGNOSTIC_PARTS`), in set order, then anything else the regime waived. Before, H14's `h14-creates-coverage` was added only when the latest candidate reached H14, so a candidate stopping earlier (no market, an early gate) took it off and the card could read a plain "On". The journal's per-decision `s0_diagnostic` still names only the parts that decision relied on. Test: `worker-1e.test.ts` (a candidate stopping at `no-market`), failing before; mutants caught: the list without the set, the set without the diagnostic on, duplicates.

  Delta review fixes (run/CI reviewer's FAIL at 62242b3, supervisor rulings):
  - B1: worker-1e's shakedown now reads the served status. With the diagnostic set it serves every waived part, and without it `[]`. Before this, serving `waived: []` passed every worker test.
  - N1: `h14-creates-coverage` counts as waived too. The strategy adds it to the served regime when H14 relies on the set, so the card never reads a plain "On" while any part of the set is waived.
  - N2(a): "Entries: On" only when an entry can pass. The status applies the entry path's per-entry R7 rule (core `evaluateEntry`): when today's loss plus one trade's worst-case costs (`maxTradeCosts`, the same network and rent inputs) reaches the daily limit, it serves `daily-loss`. Worker only; nothing under `packages/core/src/risk/**` changed.
  - N2(b), before live: in paper, "Exits: Ready" means an exit could be simulated now, not that one would land on chain. Before any live session, the card's Exits row must mean a live exit can be signed and sent (SIGN-1).
- **2026-10-04 · The app shows what the worker serves (APP-HOME).** From real use: the owner's phone read "Not started" and Home "Waiting for the data feed" while the paper worker ran, because the shell and Home used placeholders.
  - The shell's session line reads the paper worker's status: Running, Paused (the owner's pause) or Ended (session-ended). Without a status it names the reason: No server, Connecting, Offline, Not running, or Unknown (an answer that failed the checks). It never shows a state the worker did not report.
  - New endpoint `GET /api/v1/<mode>/discovered`: the strategy's candidates (the tokens it watches), at most 200, newest migration first. Each has its mint, its symbol once read (else null), its migration time, venue, pool liquidity (twice the quote reserve at the SOL price; null until both are read), and the last evaluation's token checks with their time. Checks: `failed` on a hard gate (H1–H15, H17); `missing` before an evaluation or when evidence (H16), the regime or the worker's inputs stopped it first; `passed` otherwise. Only public market data and the bot's own checks, so no new kind of stored or sent personal data; it is computed on request, and nothing is written.
  - Home lists those tokens. Columns the worker does not serve (volume, holders, top holder) show "—". Zero candidates read "No tokens discovered", with no claim about the feed. No answer shows the offline state or "Not running".
  - Snipe's Session card (the owner's screenshots at 10:42 PM: "Not started", every limit "Not set", and "Start paper session — Worker not connected" while the S0 worker ran). The paper status now carries `session`: its state (running, paused, ended) and the loaded policy's bankroll, entry and maximum entry, open positions, and daily and weekly loss limits. The trial policy has no session loss limit, so that value is null and shows "Not set". `startable` is false because the worker starts its own paper session (main.ts `startSession`), so the card offers no start button; it would appear only for a worker that reports it can accept a start. The shell and the card read the same status, polled once.
- **2026-10-05 · A newer worker reads "App update needed" (APP-COMPAT).** From real use: the owner's APK from before API-1 showed "Server error" when the worker added status fields, because the strict schema refuses unknown fields.
  - Every check stays strict. An answer with a field the app does not know is still refused and never shown.
  - The refusal's cause is now told apart. When the schema refuses an unknown field, the app re-checks the same answer once with unknown fields skipped (`onlyUnknownFields`, classification only; the answer stays refused). If nothing else fails, the refusal is `app-outdated`:
    - the connection reads "App update needed" on the Server card and the shell line;
    - the section reads "App update needed".
  - Any other refusal stays "Server error": a missing field, a bad value (also next to a new field), or an HTTP error. A real error on any endpoint outranks "App update needed", and both expire after `BAD_ANSWER_TTL_MS` as before.
  - The other direction was already handled: fields added later are `optional()`, so an older worker still loads. A test pins this.
- **2026-10-04 · App wiring (APP-WIRE).** After APP-HOME wired the shell, Home and the Session card:
  - One test runs the app's own client against the real worker's `route()` for every endpoint in all three modes (`app-wire.test.ts`). Paper passes the strict schemas; live and backtest answer "Not running"; the backtest report is empty; no path falls through to a 404.
  - The Pause control stays disabled. The app sends no commands, and pausing is the watchdog's signed `/pause`, sent from Telegram. Its hidden note reads "Telegram /pause" instead of "Worker not connected", and it reads "Paused" while the worker reports the owner's pause.
  - A worker that answers with `connected: false` reads "Feeds down", not "Worker not connected" (supervisor ruling). In api.ts, `connected` means reconciled and at least one market feed up, and the API starts only after the reconcile, so for any answer it means no market feed is connected.
  - Wallet stays as it is. A paper worker has no signing key and no bot wallet, so "Not created", "—" and "No transactions" are true. Paper's simulated balance is not shown there, because it is not a wallet.
- **2026-10-04 · Live view setup never waits unseen (OPS-1h).** This came from real use: after a successful Tailscale login, `zeroed-tailscale` sat silent. The cause is in Tailscale's CLI source (v1.104, `cmd/tailscale/cli` serve_v2.go and serve_legacy.go, `enableFeatureInteractive`):
  - `tailscale serve --https` needs the node capability `https`. Without it, serve prints a link to turn HTTPS on and blocks until it is on.
  - `tailscale funnel … off` checks the `https` and `funnel` capabilities first, even to turn Funnel off. On a tailnet without Funnel it blocks forever.
  - The script hid both outputs in /dev/null.

  The fix:
  - No funnel command in `zeroed-tailscale`. `serve --https=443` already clears Funnel for its port (`applyFunnel(false)`), `serve reset` clears it everywhere, and the existing check still confirms Funnel is off.
  - Before serving, the script checks `tailscale status --json`: it needs `CurrentTailnet.MagicDNSEnabled`, the `https` key in `Self.CapMap`, and non-empty `CertDomains`. Whatever is missing is named with the admin console's DNS page (Tailscale's documented place for both) on the console and in the paired Telegram chat, and the script exits 1.
  - Every tailscale call runs under `timeout` (`ZEROED_TS_WAIT`, default 60 s) and ends with a `Stopped:` line. The login is the exception: it keeps its own `--timeout=15m` and runs the binary directly, so the background process stays killable. `zeroed-check` bounds its two tailscale calls at 30 s.
  - serve's output is kept and shown when it fails.
  - A serve the owner made by hand is taken over by running `zeroed-tailscale` again, but only in exactly the published shape. Review B1 made `serve_ok` exact: TCP is only `{"443":{"HTTPS":true}}`, there is one Web host on :443 whose handlers are only `{"/":{"Proxy":"http://127.0.0.1:8788"}}`, no Funnel, and no Services or Foreground. Anything more (an extra path, port, host or TCP forward) is reset, and nothing stays published.
  - `zeroed-check`: if turning Funnel off fails or times out, it runs `serve reset`, removes `live_view`, and alerts the owner. The API never stays public with only an alert.
  - The e2e stand-in models both capability waits.
- **2026-10-04 · Exits per universe (CFG-2, schema version 3).** `policy.exits.universes` holds one block for each universe in use (U1, U2). S0 reads the block of the universe it controls for (U2 today). Code selects a block by the position's universe through `exitsFor`, which throws on any universe without a block; there is no default block. `EntryPlan.universe` carries the universe into `decideExit`, and `checkStopDistance` takes it as an argument.
  - **Per universe:** `tFlatMs`, `flatMinRBps`, `tMaxMs`, `partialMinShareBps`, `partialAtRBps`, `partialAtGainBps`, `atrPeriod`, `atrBarMs`, `stopAtrTenths`, and `trailAtrTenths`.
  - **Global:** the ladder (`steps`, `maxAttempts`, `maxFeePerAttempt`), `maxExitTxAtMinNotional`, `maxExitTxAboveDoubleMin`, `blockedRetryMs` and `blockedRetryAttempts`. These feed the cost reservation (`risk/evaluate.ts` reads the ladder and `blockedRetryAttempts`) and the fee reserve, so a reservation in R stays exact and does not depend on the universe. R9's thesis, flow, liquidity and quote stops also stay global: `deployerSellSupplyBps`, `negativeFlowMinutes`, `liquidityDropBps` and `reverseQuoteFailures`. This means no universe's baseline can weaken an R9 stop without a risk review (CFG-2 review). If U1 needs S2's range-return invalidation, that is a new exit trigger for a later card, not a change to R9. `deployerSellSupplyBps` stays equal to the rug label's `creatorDump.supplyBps` (`config/rugs.ts`).
  - **Values:** U2 keeps the single block's values. U1 uses risk.md S2: T_flat 30 min, ATR(14) on 5-minute bars, trail × 3.0, and 50% at +2R. Where S2 says nothing (`flatMinRBps`, `partialAtGainBps`, `stopAtrTenths`), U1 takes U2's value. U1's T_max is 120 min, the phase-1 hard maximum (§9), as the ruling says. S2's 4 h would be a separate variant that needs the owner. The cap is the global `exits.tMaxCapMs` (120 min). Validation refuses any universe's `tMaxMs` above it, and the cap may only fall by override. Validation also checks a hard phase-1 limit, `PHASE1_T_MAX_MS` (120 min), against both the cap and every universe's `tMaxMs`, so the limit never depends on the baselines alone. These are paper trial values, and the study sets the frozen ones.
  - **CFG-1 guarantees per block:** validation refuses a missing block, an unknown universe and a missing field. Cross-field checks run on each block. Tighten-only rules compare each block with the same universe's block in the baseline, so U1's T_flat may drop from 30 to 20 min, while U2's may not rise from 15 to 20 min. The hash covers every block.
  - **Open (stats):** with U1's +2R partial, the return cap is 0.4 rather than 0.3. At that cap, the demotion power check in `stats-gates.test.ts` catches a −10% decay within 30 days in 71% of runs at ρ 0, below the 80% target. That check still runs at U2's cap. U1's cap is reported to the supervisor for the stats card.

## Evidence gates (GATE-1, `packages/core/src/gates`)

- **2026-10-03 · The gates read facts only through the as-of store and judge each one before use.** Facts have fixed keys and shapes (`gates/facts.ts`). A fact is unusable, and the gate that needed it rejects under H16 with a typed reason, when it is missing, malformed, stamped after now, flagged anything other than `backfilled` or `deduplicated` (so `fork-suspect`, `provider-degraded`, `partial`, `estimated` and `rate-limited` all reject), or, for chain facts, read at `processed` commitment or without a commitment. This closes the gap noted under Evidence above.
- **2026-10-03 · Freshness is "2 slots or 2 s", from the policy (`maxStateSlotLag`, `maxQuoteAgeMs`).** Chain state read once (live `getTokenLargestAccounts`) must be within 2 slots of now. State kept current by a subscription (or the backtest dataset) names its stream: it stays fresh while that stream's head is within 2 slots of now and the stream has had no gap since the value was observed. A dataset gap therefore rejects a candidate, never fills it. Off-chain reads (simulation, third-party checks, execution health) must be at most 2 s old. Events that never change (create, migration) need only be present.
- **2026-10-03 · Live-only vetoes are absent in the backtest, as §16.3 says, not "unknown".** The GATE-1 task card said a live-only input rejects in the backtest unless §16.3 gives a proxy. §16.3 gives one for H15 (the exact local round trip) and says the H16 cross-checks and execution health are absent, with the bias written into every report, because requiring them would reject every historical candidate. The gates follow §16.3: in the backtest they are skipped and recorded as `live-only-not-applied` notes, so BT-1 can count them for the bias note. Every other input is required in both modes.
- **2026-10-03 · H6 rejects any outstanding LP token.** Migration LP is burned, so a canonical pool has no LP outstanding: the LP mint supply was 0 on all four canonical pools in DEC-1's mainnet fixtures (read 2026-10-03). Any LP outstanding is liquidity someone can withdraw. No threshold, so no new policy field.
- **2026-10-03 · H8 compares the quote side alone with the R12 floor.** Effective quote reserve (vault + signed virtual reserve) valued at the latest hourly SOL/USD point, rounded down, against max(floor, 1,000 × size), and the U1 floor for U1. This is the literal reading of H8 and half of the two-sided "liquidity" figure, so it is the stricter one. A SOL/USD point older than 2 h (two steps of an hourly series) is stale.
- **2026-10-03 · H11 needs 1-minute candles.** Any other interval is malformed: shorter candles would split a spike into small steps.
- **2026-10-03 · H11 uses the candle high.** A 1-minute candle in the last 3 minutes whose high is more than 25% above its open rejects (stricter than the close). The +5 min price (U2 only) is the close of the last candle that ended by migration + 5 min; none means unknown.
- **2026-10-03 · H12 and H13 measure shares of circulating supply, rounded up.** Circulating = supply − excluded balances. Excluded, by name only: this mint's curve accounts, the canonical pool's stored base vault (by address, not every account the pool owns), the mayhem vault (owner PDA ["sol-vault"] of `MAYHEM…`, checked equal to `BwWK17cb…`) and the incinerator. Locker accounts stay holders and are noted (review round 2): the Raydium locker escrows LP tokens and position NFTs, never a coin's own tokens, and its immutability is unproven. A locker is excluded only under a later decision with evidence: an immutable or pinned program, holding this mint's tokens, locked past our holding time. Any other PDA owner stays in the holder set and is noted, even one of pump's or PumpSwap's own programs: a non-canonical PumpSwap pool is a PumpSwap PDA whose LP owner can withdraw its tokens (review round 1; an earlier blanket "program account" exclusion let a whale hide there). The holder read's supply must equal the mint's, or H12 and H13 reject as inconsistent. Holders are grouped by owner. The dev counts in both the insider total and the dev cluster. (An earlier bound, the smallest listed balance for an unlisted insider, was replaced by GATE-1d below.)
- **2026-10-03 · H14 windows.** Serial: distinct mints by the creator with creation time in the last 24 h, this mint included, more than `serialMaxMints24h`. Rug: a prior rug the index learned of within the last `deployerRugLookbackDays` (inclusive). The index must be gap-free since at least that long ago, otherwise H14's input is not covered and rejects.
- **2026-10-03 · H15's tolerance is the integer rounding bound, so no policy field is needed now.** Live, the simulated round trip may lose at most the local model's loss plus `ROUND_TRIP_ROUNDING_LAMPORTS` (16 lamports). This is the strictest tolerance that exact math allows. A looser one would be a new policy field (CFG-1 deferred it to this card); none is added.
- **2026-10-03 · The regime gate checks on the hour, with the policy's hysteresis.** The current check is the latest hour boundary that the SOL/USD series and the graduate snapshot have both reached, and it must be within 2 h of now. Each check evaluates graduate survival (share of graduates whose +30 min mark fell in the last 24 h with effective reserves above 30 SOL, against the exact median of the 14 daily shares before), curve volume (day D−3 against the nearest-rank 25th percentile of an expanding window, see "Regime volume window") and SOL's 24 h change in basis points (above −800). Inside a snapshot, points dated after the check are ignored. The gate is off at once when the current check cannot be computed. Otherwise it is off when the last `failedChecksToDisable` checks all failed (an unknown earlier check counts as failed). Live, a missing, stale or red execution-health fact turns it off; in the backtest that input is absent.
- **2026-10-04 · GATE-1d: a partial holder view is judged at its worst case, so splitting a balance cannot hide it.** A holder can spread tokens over any number of accounts, so a largest-accounts list (`getTokenLargestAccounts`, top 20) says nothing about owners or extra accounts it does not show (the smallest-listed-balance bound was wrong: the dev at 50% in 100 small accounts read as 1.4%). Every listed account is grouped by owner as before; the rest, `unaccounted` = supply − every listed balance (vault, curve, mayhem, burn and locker accounts are listed, so they are not in it), could all belong to any one owner, listed or not. H12 and H13 first judge what is listed (real rejects stay rejects). If nothing is unaccounted they are done. Otherwise they pass only if the top holder, the top 10, the insiders and the dev cluster each stay inside their limit with all of `unaccounted` added (the dev is one of the owners, so the top-holder bound covers the dev). Circulating stays supply − listed excluded balances: if part x of the unaccounted tokens were really in an excluded account that is not listed, the true share (a + u − x)/(C − x) is never above the bound (a + u)/C, because a + u ≤ C; if any could breach, they reject with H16 `not-covered` (input `holders`, neededBy H12 or H13, value = the unaccounted amount). A complete view (`coverage: 'all'`) must add up to the supply exactly, and listed balances above the supply are inconsistent. Review of PR #50: an account listed twice, or an account whose `mint` (now carried on every holder account) is missing or another mint, makes the read H16 `malformed` (a repeat would hide unlisted supply). A complete set (GATE-1e) is one `getProgramAccounts` response at confirmed commitment, read within `maxStateSlotLag`, at or after the mint supply read (also confirmed): mint read first, holders at or after it, exact sum, mint authority none, same-fork assumption. With the mint authority none (H2), supply only falls (burns), so a scan made at or after the supply read sums to at most that supply, and an exact sum proves nothing was omitted; any difference is H16 `inconsistent`. Read the other way round, a burn equal to an omitted balance would make the sum exact while that balance is missing (second review's counterexample), so a holder set read before the mint is H16 `not-covered`. A partial (`'largest'`) view needs the same order (GATE-1f): a scan before a burn would understate the unlisted supply its worst case adds. Balances are not control (GATE-1e): every holder account carries `delegate` and `delegatedAmount` (a delegated amount without a delegate is H16 `malformed`), and in the H12/H13 numerators the delegate also counts as holding min(delegated amount, balance) of each account it may move, in addition to the owner. That only raises concentration and never enters the exact-sum check; `observedDistinctOwners` still counts owners only. Burned tokens are subtracted once: a Burn instruction already lowers the mint supply, and the only burn exclusion is balances still held by the incinerator. The top holder's worst case rejects at `> singleHolderBps` or `>= hardHolderBps`, so equal limits (allowed) cannot let it pass at the hard limit. The passing world in the tests is a complete set that adds up to the supply. Parity: the backtest rebuilds every holder of a mint from its full token-balance history (every transfer, mint and burn since creation), so it supplies `coverage: 'all'`; the live view from `getTokenLargestAccounts` is `'largest'` and, for a young coin with most supply outside the top 20, is normally not covered. Live can pass where the backtest passes only with a complete account set (FACTS-1: `getProgramAccounts` by mint, filtered to token accounts of this mint, read at the same slot as the mint's supply). Both feed the same `holders` fact and the same bound, so a live view never passes where its own data is incomplete. A decision the backtest passed on `'all'` and live rejected as not covered is a known, safe difference (live trades less), to be measured in the dry run.
- **2026-10-04 · "Independent" needs funding evidence (GATE-1d review).** The soft feature counting distinct owners in the holder read is `observedDistinctOwners`, not "independent holders": a holder list cannot show common funders. The split comes from FACTS-1, which has point-in-time funding records, as three reported counts on the soft fact: `knownLinkedOwners`, `supportedIndependentOwners` and `unresolvedOwners` (unknown when not reported). A test fails if a feature computed by the gates carries the word "independent".
- **2026-10-03 · Soft features are logged, never scored yet.** Values come from the feed's precomputed soft fact, the holder exclusions and our deployer index. Unknown values stay unknown with the reason. RugCheck's single-holder flag is logged as false when the largest account is a known vault. Third-party fields (RugCheck score and flag) are logged as live only in the backtest, whatever the feed supplies. A rule score needs thresholds that only calibration (§13) can give.
- **2026-10-03 · Evaluation order is fixed and cheapest first.** Field checks first (H1–H4, H6–H11, H14, H16), then address derivation and holder classification (H5, H12, H13), then the quote and simulation (H15). By default evaluation stops at the first failing gate; calibration runs can evaluate all. Each fact is looked up once per evaluation. Holder order in the input does not change the result.
- **2026-10-03 · GATE-1c: H5 refuses a pool whose trade events carry the upgrade's tail wrongly.** Every PumpSwap trade event of the pool released since migration (FEED-1 keys `pump_amm:BuyEvent|SellEvent:<pool>` and their `logs:` copies, read from the as-of history) must carry a tail of the length the upgrade boundary allows: 0 bytes before slot 452,654,882, 8 after it, either in that slot itself (UPG-1's upgrade transaction). The tail must also be all zeros. Otherwise H5 rejects as `event-tail`, naming the first offending signature in release order. No trade event since migration is H16 `not-covered` (input `trades`); an event without a readable tail is H16 `malformed`. The pool's tape starts at the migration event's slot (the migration fact's `obs.slot`), or at its time when it has no slot. The mint's own curve tape (`pump:TradeEvent:<mint>` and its `logs:` copy) is checked with the same rule against pump's boundary (452,654,932), over every curve event as of now; a curve tape that is not there is not a failure, because the pool tape is the required evidence (review of PR #42). Cost: H5 rescans every trade event since migration (and the curve tape) on each evaluation, so it grows with the token's age; watch it in the dry run, and cache per pool if it shows. The backtest feed must emit the same trade-event keys.
- **2026-10-03 · Time units live in `config/time.ts`.** The money-literal guard bans numbers of 1,000 or more outside `config/`, so the gates import minute, hour and day lengths from there.
- **2026-10-03 · GATE-1b: our own deployer index, fed only with released events.** `DeployerIndex` is held by the strategy and observes every market event the engine releases, so it is as of now by construction; each answer is also filtered to now. It counts each creator's mints from FEED-1's create events (`logs:pump:CreateEvent:<mint>` from the logs stream and `pump:CreateEvent:<mint>` from fetched transactions, one count per mint at its earliest block time) and rug labels from `rug:<mint>` facts `{ mint, creator }`, each dated when it was recorded. Processed-commitment creates are counted: a create that is later rolled back can only raise the count, never lower it. No rug labeller exists yet, and §7 gives no rug rule, so none is invented here. Rug labels count only once a reviewed labeller is wired by an explicit flag (`GateDeps.rugLabeller: 'RUG-1'`), never because some `rug:` fact exists, and only while its own `coverage:rugs:*` facts show it covered the whole look-back (same rules as creates). Until then every evaluation that reaches H14, live and backtest, rejects with H16 `not-covered` (neededBy H14, "rug labels unavailable (no reviewed labeller; RUG-1)"; changed by the RUG-1 review, 2026-10-04, from a note that let H14 pass), and the soft feature `indexRugs` is unknown: no labels is never read as zero rugs. The deployer-count half stays enforced (supervisor, 2026-10-03).
- **2026-10-03 · H14 checks creates coverage itself, whatever built the index.** It reads the as-of history of FEED-1's `coverage:creates:start|gap|resume` facts (several watches share each key). Coverage begins at a start, which must be reported by the look-back start. An open gap (`toSlot` null) stays uncovered until a bounded gap or a resume with the same `via` and `fromSlot`, or a new start on the same `via`. A bounded (lossy) gap reported at or after the look-back start is uncovered. A resume restores the range. Unreadable coverage facts count as open gaps. Gaps from every watch count, even if another watch was up (the safe side). Any of these rejects as H16 `not-covered` (input `coverage`), so a gap can never make H14 count fewer mints. Ranges are judged by when they were reported, because the gate has no slot-to-time map: a gap cannot end after its report. The backtest feed must emit the same coverage facts (dataset gaps as bounded gaps).
- **2026-10-03 · The index must have watched the whole look-back itself.** `DeployerIndex` records its first observed event; its `coverageFromMs` is the later of that and the stream's coverage. An index started mid-run (a restart, or one built late) has not seen the creates before it, so H14 rejects as not covered even when the stream's coverage facts say covered (review of PR #29).
- **2026-10-03 · A cut or undecodable creates log is a hole until its transaction is fetched.** FEED-1 marks a cut log (`truncated: true` on its events) and emits `logs:truncated:<via>` or `logs:undecodable:<via>` when no event could be read. On a creates watch (a `via` named by a `coverage:creates:start`), any of these inside the look-back makes H14 reject as H16 `not-covered` (input `coverage`) until a fetched-transaction event for the same signature (`ev:<signature>:…`) is released.
- **2026-10-03 · An unreadable coverage fact blocks H14 until an operator acts.** It counts as an open gap, so H14 rejects every deployer (fail safe). The operator path: fix the producer, then restart the creates watch so FEED-1 reports a new `coverage:creates:start` on that `via`; that settles the gap as lossy, and H14 passes again once the look-back (14 days) has moved past it, or at once after a SEED-1 backfill covers the range. Nothing in code clears it.
- **2026-10-03 · SEED-1 (design only, a card for WORKER-1's wave): seeding the deployer index at start-up.** With the index's own start checked, a fresh or restarted worker would reject H14 for 14 days, including the 48 h qualifying dry run. So at start-up, before the live streams are trusted, the index is seeded through `DeployerIndex.seed(events, coverage)`. `events` are create events in the same shape FEED-1 emits; `coverage` are `coverage:creates:*` facts for the seeded range, read with the same rules. Sources, oldest first: (1) DATA-1's published days of create transactions (no RPC cost), then (2) an RPC backfill from the dataset's last slot to the process start, using `getSignaturesForAddress` on the pump program's create authority and `getTransaction` at `confirmed` for each signature (about 50k creates a day, so roughly 2 calls per create: about 100k calls for a one-day catch-up, far more for longer; the scheduler runs it at P3 within the 70% credit rule and stops rather than exceed it). As-of rules: seeding happens before the engine's first decision, and every seeded event and coverage fact is dated at or before the process start, so nothing seeded is newer than the first decision moment. Seeded events count from their block time, and the index's own start becomes the seeded range's start. Backfill gaps (a page limit hit, a failed call, a slot range with no answer) are reported as bounded lossy `coverage:creates:gap` facts for that range, so a partial seed stays uncovered there and is never read as complete. The live stream's own `coverage:creates:start` then continues coverage from the process start.
- **2026-10-04 · SEED-1 built (`packages/worker/src/seed/`, `DeployerIndex.seed`).**
  - **Interface (WORKER-1).** `buildSeed({ days, rpc: { rpc, timers, provider, creditCap }, rpcFrom, fill, untilSlot, asOf })` returns `{ creates, coverage, report }`. The worker then calls `DeployerIndex.seed(creates, coverage, asOf)` before the first decision and releases `coverage` into the engine ahead of the live events: H14 reads coverage only from engine history, so a seed whose facts never reach the engine stays not-covered. Order: start the live creates watch first and buffer it; when its `coverage:creates:start` arrives, seed with `untilSlot` = that start's slot and `asOf` = now. Seed and live then meet at `untilSlot`. If the seed cannot reach it, the rest is a bounded gap, never a silent hole (`createsCoverage` has no notion of a start ending).
  - **As-of.** `seed` refuses, and changes nothing, if an event or fact is dated after `asOf`, a create's chain time is after it, events are out of release order, it is called twice, or it is called after a live event. `buildSeed` first drops a signature newer than `untilSlot` before fetching it, and any create after `asOf`. The leak test plants a future-only marker in both sources.
  - **Index start.** The index's own start becomes the seeded range's first `coverage:creates:start` (via `seed`). A seed with no start leaves it at the first live event.
  - **Days.** Assets are checked against `SHA256SUMS-DAY`, and a units part it does not list is refused. The units tar is read as one stream over its parts, skipping bodies that are not needed. Coverage is judged per unit, and a unit is covered only if it finished with no decode failure, missing meta, unknown pump event or parent-link break. Defective units, unfinished units and slot ranges between units are gaps. A day is used only when `qa-DAY.json` and `parity-DAY.json` are listed in the sums file and passed (strict QA `strict.pass`; parity with no mismatch and no missing row, the backtest's `failed()` rule). Create rows outside a sampled tape have no raw record, so they are decoded by the scanner, not DEC-1; they are accepted only from days whose QA and parity passed, until DATA-1 keeps raw records for every create transaction (asked by the supervisor, about 50 MB a day). A missing, altered or failed day never stops the seed: it is reported in `report.days` with its error and dropped, so its range becomes a gap (or the RPC backfill's, when it was the last day). Days on or after 2026-10-02 are refused, and a unit that crosses the boundary is cut at its last block before it (from its `blocks.csv.zst`; refused without one).
  - **RPC.** `getSignaturesForAddress` on pump's mint authority `TSLvdd1pWpHVjahSpsvCXUbgwsL3JAcvokwaKt1eokM` (every successful one checked on mainnet was a `CreateV2`), pages of 1,000, newest first. Then `getTransaction` at `confirmed`, decoded only by DEC-1's `transactionEvents`, with ids and keys equal to FEED-1's `eventsOfFrame` for the same transaction (tested on DEC-1's real create fixtures). Calls run at P3 through the provider's scheduler (the 70% halt) and inside the run's own `rpc.creditCap`, each charged the scheduler's `callCost(provider, method)`. The first page carries `minContextSlot` = `untilSlot`, and the node's -32016 answer (confirmed is not there yet) backs off and retries, so the last processed-only slots before the live start are not missed; if confirmed never gets there, the run stops `not-confirmed` with everything owed as a gap. The run is done only once a signature at or below the range's first slot was seen; a short or empty page before that stops it as `history-end` with the rest a gap. Rate limits, timeouts, network errors, 5xx and refused queue slots back off at 2, 4 and 8 s (capped at 16 s), 4 attempts per call. A transaction that cannot be read, or has no decodable create, is a one-slot gap. 20 such in a row, a failed signature page, the cap or the halt stop the run, and everything not done becomes one bounded gap. Newest first means a stopped run's gap sits at the old end, which leaves H14's window first.
  - **Gap dating.** Each gap is dated at its last block time, or the next known one, never earlier. If neither is known it is dated at `asOf`, the latest possible and so the safe side. Overlapping gaps merge.
  - **Cost, measured 2026-10-04.** On the public RPC, 1,000 create-authority signatures took 1,254 s and 7.2% failed: about 69,000 a day, about 64,000 creates. `estimateBackfillCredits` gives about **64,100 Helius credits per day of catch-up**, which is 6.4% of the free month. 14 days is about 897,000, more than the 700,000 halt. Published days end on 1 Oct, so from about 15 Oct a full look-back cannot be seeded on the free plan. The run stops at its cap or the halt, and the old end stays a gap. `rpc.creditCap` (accepted by the supervisor): 150,000 (15% of the month, about 2.3 days of catch-up), so live traffic keeps its share. The report carries the estimate, the cap, `fitsCap`, the calls, the credits used, retries, futures dropped and why the run stopped.
  - **Rugs are not seeded (blocker for the dry run, sent to the supervisor 2026-10-04).** Day releases keep trades only for the 5% hash sample, and an RPC trade backfill for every mint costs millions of credits a day. So no `coverage:rugs:*` fact is produced, and under RUG-1's rule the rug half of H14 is not covered for 14 days after any start or restart. Options sent to the supervisor: an on-demand rug backfill per deployer (only the creator's prior mints, a few dozen credits a decision), or saving the index, labeller and coverage state across restarts.
  - **Restarts: saved state plus a downtime fill (supervisor ruling, 2026-10-04).** WORKER-1 saves the deployer index, the rug labeller's state and the coverage facts across restarts (public chain data; the supervisor approved the shape). On restart, `buildSeed({ days: [], fill: { fromSlot, fromMs, close }, rpc, untilSlot, asOf })` backfills only the downtime. It makes no start fact, because the saved coverage continues. Every unfetched slot is a bounded gap on via `seed`. `close` names the saved watch's open gap (`via`, `fromSlot`). The fill range is `fill.fromSlot`..`untilSlot`. A complete fill closes it with a `coverage:creates:resume` up to `untilSlot`, so coverage stays continuous; an incomplete fill closes it with a bounded gap on the same `via` and `fromSlot` (lossy). The close is dated from the restarted watch's actual `coverage:creates:start` fact, which the worker passes as `fill.liveStart`: same slot and in-slot position, at `min(fill.fromMs, liveStart.receivedAt − 1)`, so `createsCoverage`'s order puts it first and that start cannot settle the saved open gap as lossy before it. The feed may place the start below `untilSlot` (after a skipped slot, a socket ahead of the feed tip, or at `released + 1` with no tip), so no slot derived from `untilSlot` is safe. A close without `liveStart` is refused. Tests cover a start at `untilSlot`, one and two slots below it, at `released + 1`, and timed at the downtime's first block (#45 re-reviews). The fill reads from the older of `fill.fromSlot` and `close.fromSlot`, because the close restores the saved gap from `close.fromSlot` and FEED-1's gap starts at the old watch's last log slot, inclusive (#45 re-review: reading more beats refusing). It is empty and complete (no RPC call, a resume) only when `close.fromSlot` is after `untilSlot`, or is null and `fill.fromSlot` is after it (a failover, or a node behind the one that saved the state): the saved coverage then already reaches past the new start. `close.at` is the moment the saved gap was reported. A saved gap reported at or after the restarted start is not settled by that start, so the close then follows the gap by 1 ms; without `close.at` such a gap stays open, which is fail safe (tested). A `liveStart` or `close.at` dated after `asOf` is refused, as `seed` refuses its facts, so no coverage fact is ever dated after the process start. A close that would still land after `asOf` (a `close.at` exactly at it, followed by 1 ms) is not made, so the saved gap stays open (fail safe). Without a fill, the restarted watch's new start settles the open gap as lossy, so H14 is not covered (tested). WORKER-1 must run the fill on every restart, with no RPC if need be: a crash leaves no open gap, and only the fill's gaps keep the downtime from reading as covered. Fill creates go to the restored index through `DeployerIndex.fill(creates, asOf)`, which accepts creates older than live events already seen, with the same as-of and order checks as `seed`, all or nothing, and never moves the index's start. The fill's coverage goes into the engine before the watch's new start (test: restart, fill, coverage continuous). A few minutes of downtime costs a few hundred credits.
  - **Rug half (supervisor ruling).** RUG-1c builds an on-demand rug check per deployer, live and in the backtest. SEED-1 still emits no `coverage:rugs:*` and says so in its report.
  - **Evidence.** No `data-day-*` release existed on 2026-10-04, so the day source runs on a synthetic release in the published layout (tar split mid-entry, `SHA256SUMS-DAY`), and the RPC source runs on DEC-1's real mainnet creates from 2026-10-03. Tests: gap stays gap, leak, boundary, sums, retry and backoff, page failure, credit-cap exhaustion, the real scheduler's 70% halt, restart re-seed (identical output, a later restart extends it) and the end-to-end H14 coverage. All 26 mutations of the guards (review round included) fail the tests. Still to do: one run against the first published day.
- **2026-10-03 · The create fact can come from FEED-1.** When no `gates/create` fact exists, H9, H12, H13 and H14 read FEED-1's create from a fetched transaction (confirmed) or, failing that, from the logs stream. The logs stream is subscribed at `processed`, so a create known only from logs is refused (H16 degraded) until the transaction is fetched at `confirmed`.

## Funding screens (FUND-1, `apps/web/src/funding`)

- **2026-10-03 · What can be withdrawn.** Balance minus the protected SOL reserve (§8 R4, from the worker) minus one transfer fee (5,000 lamports), never below zero. The fee comes off the amount that can be sent so a full send never dips below the reserve. SOL amounts are decimal strings with up to 9 places and all arithmetic is bigint lamports; AUD values use the SOL/AUD price string and round to cents.
- **2026-10-03 · One destination.** The Withdraw form refuses any address that is not exactly the saved wallet, and a malformed one is reported as malformed. A saved-wallet change is a separate request that takes effect 24 hours later; until then Withdraw keeps the old address. Both flows need the passkey step-up first. The app wires a step-up that always refuses (`unavailableStepUp`) until the signer work provides the real passkey check, so no request can be created by accident; the sample-data page uses an approving stub.
- **2026-10-03 · QR code uses `qrcode-generator` 2.0.4; `jsqr` 1.4.0 is test-only.** Supervisor approval under AGENTS.md (package.json). `qrcode-generator`: MIT, no dependencies, no install scripts, exact pin in apps/web `dependencies`; it encodes byte mode at level M and the app draws the matrix as an SVG, so there is no canvas and no network call. Reason: Reed-Solomon and mask rules are easy to get subtly wrong, and a wrong QR on a funding screen is worse than a pinned dependency. (A hand-written encoder was built first and replaced on the supervisor's ruling.) `jsqr`: Apache-2.0, no dependencies, no install scripts, exact pin in `devDependencies`, imported only by `test/qr.test.ts`, never bundled. `test/qr.test.ts` draws the matrix with a quiet zone, decodes it with jsQR and requires the exact address for 32 to 44 character addresses, requires a one-character change to change the decoded value, checks level M and byte mode, and proves the shown address, the QR code and the copy button carry the same value (the button copies from `data-copy`, set from the same variable as the text and the QR). The code is drawn black on white in both themes because scanners read dark on light.
- **2026-10-03 · Requirements for SIGN-1.** (a) After the passkey step-up, the signer path re-checks the destination against the latest saved wallet and the amount against the latest balance and reserve, and the signer re-checks both again; the screen's check is a convenience, not the control. (b) The 24-hour saved-wallet change is shown but not yet stored or enforced: nothing persists a pending change or applies it after 24 hours. Persisting it is a new saved-data shape, so it needs the owner's approval together with SIGN-1.
- **2026-10-03 · A throwing step-up counts as a refusal.** The step-up runs inside a promise chain, so a synchronous throw, a rejection or any answer other than `true` all mean "not approved", and the busy flag is cleared in a `finally`.
- **2026-10-03 · Deposit shows no address before the gate passes.** Kept from WEB-1 (CLAUDE.md, "No deposit before proof"): until the pre-funding gate passes, Deposit shows "Not passed" and no address or QR. The sample-data page passes the gate.

## Transactions and landing (TX-1, `packages/core/src/tx`)

- **2026-10-03 · Wallet-owned accounts and transfer destinations are always static keys.** Venue and infrastructure accounts may load from a lookup table; the wallet, its token accounts, its volume accumulators and the tip account never do. The signer cannot check what a table resolves to, so the policy refuses any of those loaded from one.
- **2026-10-03 · The signer policy recomputes the worst-case SOL out from the decoded bytes.** Base fee, priority fee (from the compute-budget instructions), every transfer out, the curve buy's spend, the larger of the wrap transfers and the PumpSwap buy's `spendable_quote_in` (wrapped SOL already in the wallet's ATA would be spent too; anyone can send some), and each account the swap may create or top up, charged at the rent of the largest such account (170 bytes) at the live rate, never a caller's figure. An account created and closed in the same transaction is not charged. The builder's own itemised figure must never exceed it (review of PR #20).
- **2026-10-03 · A transaction that names any account twice is refused,** by the policy and by DEC-1's `accountKeys`, as the runtime refuses it (AccountLoadedTwice) (review of PR #20).
- **2026-10-03 · A buy quote carries the spend it was computed for; it must equal the request's spend, with at most 3 lamports left unspent by fee rounding** (measured 0 on 40,000 random quotes). A quote made for a smaller spend would size min-out for the wrong trade (review of PR #20).
- **2026-10-03 · The PumpSwap buyback recipient's quote ATA is charged when not read as existing.** All eight exist on chain (slot 452960110) and the IDL does not list them as created, but the program is not open source.
- **2026-10-03 · Rent is charged at SIMD-0437 rates read live.** Token-2022 pump ATAs are sized 170 bytes, SPL accounts 165, volume accumulators 137; an old bonding curve is charged for growth to 151 bytes (pump-sdk 2.0.0; the pinned BUY.md says 115, the larger is used).
- **2026-10-03 · The jitodontfront marker rides on the tip transfer, read-only.** The System program ignores extra accounts; venue programs parse their remaining accounts by position, so the marker is never added to a swap.
- **2026-10-03 · Compute limits come from a calibration table (p99 × 1.1, at least 20 samples).** The provisional table is built from 60 real mainnet swaps per kind with only our instruction set; TEST-2 replaces it with our own simulations. A shape missing from the table refuses the build. A priority fee that rounds to a zero unit price is refused, since Sender requires a price instruction.
