## PM-01 pre-registration (2026-10-08, researcher proposals; supervisor rulings 8 Oct 2026, 9:26–9:40 AM)

From `research/pm01/PREREG.md` §12. All five were ruled by the supervisor on 8 Oct 2026 at 9:26 AM (`docs/reviews/PM01-PREREG.md` on `claude/supervisor-docs`); the PREREG applies them (§13). Round 2 (supervisor, 8 Oct 9:30 AM): the P1 split, B1 economic, a boundary in `W_R` or `W_P` voids `W_B`'s selection, and the kill-only screen on the B-10 days (owner's message "Ok", 8 Oct 2026, 9:28 AM) fixed in PREREG §6.4. Screen rulings (9:32 AM): at least 100 closed trades per config; kill per config (a config whose 95% upper bound is below 0 is dropped, PM-01 stops when both are dropped, both still count in the trial budget); fees per trade at the lower of the day's chain-read fee and the current schedule; a `W_B` rerun after a boundary may need more than 30 days. Round 4 (supervisor, 8 Oct 9:40 AM; rulings 1–20 in `docs/reviews/PM01-PREREG.md`): a dropped config runs count-only in `W_B` for B-3 and B-5, and B-4 then becomes the halves check; `W_B` ends at ≥ 30 counted days and ≥ 300 trades for every config; no peeking in `W_R` and `W_P`, boundaries only from L-4's flag, at most one restart; the screen kills only on all trades and on the subset without cannot-sell, collapse or `no_data` exits, on 1 Hz as-of bars, and starts only after the §12 "First run" rows are merged; B-6 on the cluster t; effective size ≥ 20 days with no day over 10%; benchmark before and after the signal; named watched programs; monthly cost at the recorded daily SOL/USD; B-9 and B-10 never kill; plus the minors. Applied in PREREG §2–§13. Round 5 (supervisor, 8 Oct 9:47 AM; rulings 21–35): the voided-`W_R` kill on the lean row and the kill-side interval; benchmark side floors (`pending_data`, never a kill); the screen starts only after P12 and the listed §12 rows; calendar days for split points, counted days for counts; re-runs reproduce every decision or burn the window; monthly figure frozen per window; isolated configs; a frozen-parameter block with its sha256; kill rule 4 with effective size; as-of tie-break; pinned program IDs and economic fields; P-9's implied bankroll; config id in the RNG key; B-8 weeks need ≥ 10 trades. Round 6 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:07 PM; R6-36 to R6-45): `engine` and `gates` in the frozen block and `configKey` over it and the referenced file hashes; a mechanical decoder-only test; fee-only changes continue the window at the higher fee; kill rule 4's clock from the first `W_B` start; the monthly row hashed per window; a recorded kill stands; `fee_recipient`, `create_v2_enabled` and `whitelist_pda` hashed; `burn_percent` excluded; VERIFY items in the start condition. Round 7 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:13 PM; R7-46 to R7-55): two keys (`pm01FrozenKey` and the engine's A-M13-02 `configKey`); a five-part decoder-only test; a pending boundary pauses counts for one day; fee-only limited to protocol and LP rates and tiers; file hashes recomputed from disk; ARCH limit values in the block; the monthly figure copied into the run bundle; a kill stands unless the failing gate passes when recomputed with the fix; `whitelist_pda` always economic; kill rule 4 at 90 counted or 120 calendar days. Round 8 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:18 PM; R8-56 to R8-62): gate values loaded from the merged block and `pm01FrozenKey` checked against the `preRegister` record; fee-only fields named; pending to the end of the first full UTC day; per-trade account check and post-upgrade IDL; a key mapping table; `FEEDAY` not scaled; `killRule4MaxCalendarDays` in the block. Round 9 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:23 PM; R9-63 to R9-67): append-only `preRegister` entries; `stable_fee_tiers` and `exotic_flat_fees` named; part 5 on the pre-upgrade program bytes in a local simulator (fail closed); refreshed IDL sources in order; acceptance fixtures per kill rule and `pending_data` path. Round 10 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:28 PM; R10-68 to R10-72): part 5 from transaction data with a pinned account-creation rule table (replacing R9-65); `preRegister` ordered by append sequence; the first `W_B` trade count is the first run if the screen has not run; a published IDL dated by this repo's first fetch; combination fixtures. Round 11 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:34 PM; R11-73 to R11-80): post-UPG-1 IDLs pinned before the first run, B5 relabelled economic; a named forward-window transaction source; part 7 for program-side reserve changes (factor 2 in the block); created-account rent rule; Token-2022 ATAs 170 bytes; accounts resolved from golden fixtures; the screen after an early first run is stop-only; the first-run record checks the merged head. Round 12 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:39 PM; R12-81 to R12-88): new swap instructions tested by decoding and role mapping (B5 not yet tested, counted economic); part 7 on normalised ratios with a KS test and a 10% median tolerance; a wider fallback sample; `preRegister` entries carry the file's blob sha; the forward-source choice recorded, the last option put to the owner; `fallbackSwapCap` in the block. Round 13 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:42 PM; R13-89 to R13-95): part 7 pooled per ratio type with a Bonferroni KS test and a Poisson low-count path, size rule on median, mean or p90, look-back bounded by the previous boundary, `fallbackCurveTradeCap` in the block, stricter role mapping, B5 counted economic and not tested, pinned part 7 rules and B-10 event counts as start items. Round 14 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:45 PM; R14-96 to R14-99): low-count size rule on the rate; "new" means never seen; unlisted reserve-changing types compared on rate and size; ratios over matching spans. Round 15 (supervisor, 8 Oct 3:48 PM; R15-100, R15-101): zero-denominator events counted apart by the Poisson path; unlisted types' reserve deltas normalised by activity over the same span.

- **PM01-P1 · PM `dump_flag` baseline (ruled: accepted, 8 Oct 9:26 AM).** M08's `dumpFlagState` (A-M08-02 step 5) takes its baseline from `now − 6 h` to `now − 30 min` with 50% coverage, which a pool at most 120 min old never has, so every PM-01 decision would be `insufficient` → `error` and PM-01 could never enter. Proposal: for PM pools the baseline runs from the first complete bar after migration to `now − 30 min`, with the same 50% coverage over that span and a span of at least 10 min; the 30-min dump window and the −4 × MAD rule are unchanged. Effect: PM-01's first valid decision is at migration + 40 min, not 20. Needs an M08 change with its own `affectsReturns` key. Ruling: a PM-01-only rule fixed in the PREREG; baseline from migration to `now − 30 min` with at least 10 min of data; other strategies keep the 6 h baseline; under 10 min stays `insufficient`; the 50% coverage rule is kept over the new span; the report splits entries at 60 min of baseline (entry at migration + 90 min or later against earlier) and gives both lines, while the gates use all entries (9:30 AM).
- **PM01-P2 · Random-entry matching for PM (ruled: accepted, 8 Oct 9:26 AM).** C-65 matches on the 6 h MAD decile and drops candidates overlapping `[signal − L, signal]` with MR's `L`; neither exists for PM. Proposal: MAD of 15 s returns from the first post-migration bar to the candidate bar, deciles over all candidate bars of the same gate window (`W_B` or `W_R`; R4-10, R5-30); `L` = 60 s. Pool and UTC hour as C-65.
- **PM01-P3 · Reported sizes above today's ceilings (ruled: accepted, 8 Oct 9:26 AM).** A21's stressed gap prior (about 8,270 bps) and `MAXRISK`'s 50,000,000-lamport ceiling cap a PM-01 trade at about 57,000,000 lamports (DERIVED, approximate). Proposal: gates decide at $5 (33,333,333 lamports) with `E_bt` = 20 SOL on the live-small profile; the $20 to $10,000 lines run with `E_bt` = 600 × size and lamport ceilings scaled by size ÷ $5, reported only, never gate inputs, and never a change to the bot's ceilings (owner only). `DEPTHPCT` is never scaled. Each reported line shows gross return, fixed costs, percentage fees and price impact apart.
- **PM01-P4 · Market boundary inside `W_B` or `W_R` (ruled: accepted, 8 Oct 9:26 AM).** Trades crossing an L-4-type boundary are dropped and each side reported. Proposal: a boundary that changes trade economics (fees, reserves maths, a new mandatory account) ends the window and the counts restart after it; a decoder-only boundary (B5-type) is reported and the window continues. Ruling: economic = any fee change, a change to the reserves or pricing maths, a new mandatory account or cost-changing instruction, a change in who is paid a fee, a `disable_flags` change, a rent-per-byte change (B1–B4); decoder-only = layout-only changes (B5, while UPG-1's "economics unchanged" stands); a boundary not shown to be decoder-only within one day counts as economic. An economic boundary inside `W_R` or `W_P` ends that window and voids `W_B`'s selection; a new `W_B` selection runs after the boundary and counts as new trials (9:30 AM). Table in PREREG §6.2.
- **PM01-P5 · K3 retention horizon (ruled: accepted as the proposal for the OF-3 step, 8 Oct 9:26 AM).** PM-01's longest hold ends before migration + 240 min (last entry before + 120 min, time stop 120 min). Proposal: K3 keeps each listed pool's raw records from migration to migration + 300 min (60 min for the exit ladder); a position still open then closes by the `no_data` rule. The supervisor records it with the measured sizes and the OF-3 retention choice before batch 3.

## Z05 UI system, round 2 (2026-10-08, supervisor rulings for the round 1 review and red team)

Rulings in `docs/reviews/Z05.md` on `claude/supervisor-docs-2` at `d95eb80f`. The card's deviations from UI.md are written back under "Built in Z05" in `docs/blueprint/UI.md`.

- **Clock skew and paused data** (red team M1, M2; rulings 1, 2). Data ahead of the server clock beyond the 1 s tolerance is stale ("clock skew"), never live. Paused and disconnected keep the age state. `blockedReason` blocks clock skew, paused updates, and stale data under paused or disconnected.
- **Dialogs fail closed** (red team M3, m3, reviewer m2; rulings 3, 7). A money-affecting confirm needs a connected stream and a known mode, and stays disabled in the render where the mode changed. A change to or from an unknown mode is a mode change. HALT is exempt: it reduces risk (UI.md HALT flow).
- **No commas in SOL amounts** (red team M4; ruling 4). `0,250` and `1,500` are refused, the parsed value is always shown back, and out-of-range or over-limit values are never handed on.
- **Copy guard reads like a reader** (red team m1; ruling 5). NFKC, format characters removed, look-alike letters mapped to Latin, AI in any case, split strings, the built bundle and the rendered pages. The banned list only grows. One reviewed exact string is allowed in the bundle: React DOM's `seamless` attribute name.
- **Malformed times and the hold gesture** (red team m2, m4, reviewer m3; rulings 6, 8). A malformed `asOf` shows "as of an unknown time", never a throw. A hold is cancelled on touch cancel, lost capture, leaving the button, or a move of more than 10 px. Only a press that starts a hold claims the next click, for at most 1 s.
- **Production headers** (red team m5; ruling 9). Only the development and test server sends the CSP and security headers today. The production server must send the same headers, tested in the card that builds it.
- **Fixtures and audit records** (reviewer m1, m4; rulings 10, 11). `replay-paused.json` carries every VM-03 field. UI.md U-04 records lucide-react 1.47.0, the audited version.

## Z05 UI system, round 4 (2026-10-08, supervisor rulings 17–20)

- **Copy guard reads every confusable** (ruling 17). Every single-letter skeleton in confusables.txt 18.0.0 is mapped to Latin, before and after NFKC. Combining marks are removed, and text is read with l-like letters as both `l` and `I`. Plain `l` and `I` keep their letters. The table's generator and both pinned inputs (confusables.txt and UnicodeData.txt 18.0.0, with their sha256) are committed, and a test checks the table against the generator (ruling 20).
- **HOLD claim expires after a move** (ruling 18). A press cancelled by a move or by leaving the button keeps its click claim for 1 s, as after a pointer up. A pointer lifted outside the button then never swallows a later click.
- **Only HALT's secondary can be risk-reducing** (ruling 19). The type refuses the mark on other dialogs, and the runtime ignores it there. In the render where the mode changed, every action is disabled except HALT's confirm. Cancel stays enabled: it changes nothing.

## Z05 UI system, round 5 (2026-10-08, supervisor rulings 21–25)

- **Copy guard scope** (ruling 21). The last round of look-alike work. Dashes read as `-`, apostrophe look-alikes as `'`, white space collapses, and enclosing marks and spacing marks between Latin letters are dropped. ASCII `l`, `I`, `1` and `|` are read both ways, and `rn`, `vv` and `cl` are read as `m`, `w` and `d`. With this, the guard covers accidental wording in our own copy; deliberate look-alike hunting is out of scope. Reading `1` as `I` made the table demo's row id `A1` read as AI. The demo ids became `R1`–`R5`: they are data, not copy. Ruling 25 adds seeded tests for the second look-alike pass, the one after NFKC/NFD (`rόbust`, `ΆI picks`, `smΆrt`); removing that pass fails them.
- **HOLD claim while the pointer is down** (ruling 22, replacing ruling 18's expiry from the move). The claim's 1 s expiry starts only from the pointer up, on the button or caught by a window listener. A drag held longer than 1 s and released on the button opens nothing.
- **HaltDialog owns the exemption** (ruling 23). Only `HaltDialog` is exempt from the fail-closed gates, and it fixes its title and its confirm (`onHalt`). The generic `Dialog` overrides any `halt` key it is given.
- **HALT closes on a mode change** (ruling 24). UI.md's UI-T07 edge case closes the open dialog on a mode change, and it names no exception, so HALT closes too. UI.md now says the operator reopens HALT from the header (hold, `Enter` or `⇧H`), which is never blocked.

## Z05 UI system, round 6 (2026-10-08, supervisor rulings 26–29)

- **`1` is not read as `I`** (ruling 26, narrowing ruling 21). The action classes A1, A2 and A3 (UI.md) are copy, so `A1` must stay honest. Only ASCII `l`, `I` and `|` are read both ways. The table demo keeps R1–R5.
- **A pointer cancel anywhere ends the HOLD claim** (ruling 27). The window listener of ruling 22 also handles `pointercancel` for the same pointer, so a touch cancelled off the button never swallows a later click.
- **No risk-reducing secondary** (ruling 28, replacing rulings 15's exemption and 19). Every secondary action is gated; "Halt and flatten all…" is A2. HaltDialog's `onHalt` is a branded `HaltCommand` that only `src/lib/halt-command.ts` makes.
- **Text is also read as written** (ruling 29, fixing a weakening from ruling 21). Reading every ASCII `l` and `I` the same way missed words that hold both, like "Intelligent" and "In plain words". The guard now reads text as written as well as in the `l` and `I` readings. A false-positive check over dwyl's words_alpha.txt (370,105 words, sha256 `3ed0c946…`), each word lower-case, capitalised and upper-case, shows 174 hits. Against round 5 the only new ones are Intelligence, Intelligent and Intelligently, which are banned words. Against round 4 the extra hits are `Al`, `al` and `LIM`, read as AI and LLM; ruling 21 accepted those.

## Z03 read path and decoders (2026-10-08, builder decisions for review)

Card Z03 ports Snipe-solana C03 (#6 @ `6ae4d62`) with its SPEC-A rulings, review fixes and red-team fixes; the C03 PR description's "Ambiguity decisions" 1-15 carry over unchanged. These are the decisions the port onto Z02 needed; each is open to the reviewer.

- **Z03-1 · No lockfile overrides, so `undici-types` 8.11.0 instead of 8.10.2.** C03 pinned `ws` 8.21.3 and `undici-types` 8.10.2 under `@solana/kit` with npm `overrides`; this repository refuses overrides (DEPENDENCIES.md policy, `E_LOCK_*`). pnpm's 14-day rule resolves `ws` to 8.21.3 (8.22.0, 2026-09-26, is younger) and `undici-types` to 8.11.0 (2026-09-22). `undici-types` is type declarations only. Every other package and version equals C03's lockfile. Needs the supervisor's OK (card: versions other than C03's).
- **Z03-2 · `limits.ownerMaxRps`.** SPEC-A A-M14-02 (as edited after C03) asks that "a Chainstack config of 2.5 req/s" runs its bucket at 0.5 req/s. A provider may name an owner cap; the registry runs every rate of that provider at no more than it, and the 50% check still reads the configured rates.
- **Z03-3 · `rpc.providers_file`.** The provider list is objects, which the frozen `ConfigFieldSchema` types cannot hold, so M25 holds the path of a root-owned JSON file (`/etc/bot/rpc-providers.json`) that `loadProviders` validates; M26 reads it at start (not built yet). `rpc.max_response_bytes`, `rpc.p0_reserve_bps`, `rpc.default_timeout_ms.p0`..`p4` and `rpc.send_enabled` (off; raises risk; live modes only) are M25 fields.
- **Z03-4 · Default providers.** `DEFAULT_PROVIDERS` holds Shyft Free (5 of 10 req/s [VF-09], unmetered primary) and Chainstack Developer (2.5 of 5 req/s [VF-10], owner cap 0.5 req/s). Helius is left out: the owner's rule of 2026-10-07 keeps its headroom unused in Phase 0. No default has the send role.
- **Z03-5 · Log codes in lower case.** C03 wrote `M14.*`, `M02.*`, `M01.*`; M27's code rule is `module.event` in lower case (as M24, M25 and M27 use), and the logger writes any other code as `m27.unknown_code`. The codes are `m14.*`, `m02.*`, `m01.*`, each with its declared fields, so no field is redacted by mistake.
- **Z03-6 · `MetricsPort` typed by the M27 catalog.** The gateway, client and decoders take M27's `MetricsRegistry` as their port, and their metric names are in the catalog (`rpc_latency_ms`, `rpc_queue_depth`, `rpc_wait_ms`, `rpc_failover_total`, `rpc_429_total`, `rpc_byte_budget_refused_total`, `decode_accounts_total`, `decode_events_total`, `decode_gap_total`).
- **Z03-7 · C11's capture tools are not ported.** Only C11's data (`fixtures/mainnet`, MANIFEST checked) came over; `tools/fixtures/*` read the network and are not needed by any test. The program-ID lint rule therefore exempts only `packages/venue/src/constants.ts`.
- **Z03-8 · DEC-1 goldens.** `fixtures/dec1` holds DEC-1's 29 mainnet accounts and 22 transactions and DEC-1's decoded values renamed to the Z03 vocabulary (`fixtures/dec1/README.md`); the Z03 decoders agree on every field (0 mismatches). It adds cases C03 lacked: a real non-canonical pool, a mayhem coin, a 2024-layout curve, negative virtual quote reserves and the 8 bytes appended on 2026-10-02.

### Z03 round 2 (2026-10-08, builder decisions under the supervisor's rulings 1–12, `docs/reviews/Z03.md`)

- **Z03-9 · Process model and the 50% budget (ruling m12).** Three processes read RPC providers: the engine, the sentinel (M29: its own balance read, ARCH 11.1 "Sentinel balance read") and the signer (M17: its reserved Chainstack and Helius blocks, ARCH D04; SPEC-A A-M14-05 "Allocations, credits and rate"). So the budget is split by config, not held by one process: `loadProviders` takes an `Allocation` (each provider's shares per consumer in basis points of its 50%-of-documented budget, and the consumer this process is), refuses shares above 10,000 in total (`allocation_exceeds_cap`, SPEC-A `E_ALLOCATION_EXCEEDS_CAP`), scales every rate of a provider by this process's share, and does not use a provider with no share for it. The engine context refuses to load without an allocation. Reading `/etc/bot/rpc-allocation.json` and the credit split stay with A-M14-05 (Z08). Deliberate string building of an address at run time is out of the lint rule's reach (ruling m7); review and the red team catch it.
- **Z03-10 · The inner-instruction ordinal without a `@bot/types` bump (ruling m11).** `DecodedEvent` lives in the frozen `@bot/types` 1.0.0, whose contract rule needs a version bump and both group leads' sign-off lines in its CHANGELOG; those sign-offs were the owner's, and a builder cannot write them. So the ordinal travels beside the event: `decodeTransactionEventsLocated` returns `{ event, outerIx, innerIx, layoutExtended }`, and `decodeTransactionEvents` keeps its `DecodedEvent[]` shape. Moving the ordinal into `DecodedEvent` is a later `@bot/types` change with the owner's sign-off, if a consumer needs it there.
- **Z03-11 · A PumpSwap trade's quote mint (ruling 3).** A `BuyEvent` or `SellEvent` names its pool but not the pool's quote mint, and a transaction does not carry the pool account. The decoder reads the `quote_mint` account of the PumpSwap instruction that invoked the event-CPI: the pinned `pump_amm.json` marks it `relations: ["pool"]` on `buy`, `buy_exact_quote_in` and `sell` (Anchor `has_one`: the program refuses an instruction whose `quote_mint` is not the pool's), so it is the pool account's own quote mint. The wSOL mint comes from A-M01-01 (`MINTS.wsol`) through `DecodersOptions.wsolMint`; without it every PumpSwap trade is refused. On DEC-1's mainnet sample, 4 of 11 PumpSwap trades and 1 of 9 curve trades are not SOL-quoted; before round 2 they were reported as Lamports (`fixtures/dec1/README.md`).
- **Z03-12 · The 8 bytes after pump's events (ruling 2).** VERIFIED: `pump-fun/pump-public-docs` commit `8cda1fa30ea658b20909d8aedf002047119388d2` (2026-10-08, "v3 / v2 trades, multi-hop swap, pump coins as quote mints, fee sweeps, synthetic migration") appends `creator_fee_unclaimed: u64` to `TradeEvent` (idl/pump.json) and to `BuyEvent` and `SellEvent` (idl/pump_amm.json); every other field of the six mapped events is unchanged against the pinned `cb188ce`. DEC-1 saw the 8 bytes on mainnet from 2026-10-02 (DECISIONS 2026-10-03). The pin stays at `cb188ce` (a newer IDL is pinned only in its own reviewed change); until then such events carry `layoutExtended` and count in `decode_layout_extended_total{kind}`. The same commit adds new instructions and events (`PostCompleteBuyEvent`, `SweepPoolFeeEvent` and others) that the pinned IDL decodes as `unknown_event`.
- **Z03-13 · Served methods and JSON-RPC rate-limit codes (ruling 4).** Each provider names the methods it serves and the JSON-RPC codes it documents for rate limits (sources in `packages/engine/src/m14/defaults.ts`, read 2026-10-08, no live call): Shyft Free serves no index method (0 index req/s) and documents HTTP 429 only; Chainstack Developer serves no `getProgramAccounts` (paid plans) and documents HTTP 429 with JSON-RPC `-32005`. Neither serves Helius's `getPriorityFeeEstimate`. A method no eligible provider serves fails with `E_RPC method_not_served` and sends nothing.

### Z03 round 3 (2026-10-08, rulings 13–16)

- **Z03-14 · -32005 is a lagging node, not a rate limit (ruling 13; replaces Z03-13's Chainstack entry).** VERIFIED: anza-xyz/agave `rpc-client-api/src/custom_error.rs` at `4cd046d7fea12e330bbab0af9f0b019650e8afbe` (read 2026-10-08) defines `JSON_RPC_SERVER_ERROR_NODE_UNHEALTHY: i64 = -32005`, message "Node is behind by N slots" or "Node is unhealthy". Chainstack's documented over-limit answer is HTTP 429 with -32005, and the 429 status alone makes it a rate limit. A provider's `rateLimitRpcErrors` now names an exact code and message; Chainstack and Shyft list none. An HTTP 200 -32005 fails a P0/P1 read over and never counts toward the stop rule.
- **Z03-15 · The trusted quote-mint instructions (ruling 14).** `PinnedIdl.poolQuoteMint` is built when the IDL loads: every pump_amm instruction whose `quote_mint` account carries `relations: ["pool"]` (at `cb188ce`: `buy`, `buy_exact_quote_in`, `sell`, `deposit`, `withdraw`, `init_boost`, `boost_buy_and_burn`). An event invoked by any other instruction, `create_pool` included, gets no quote mint and is refused.
- **Z03-16 · Byte budgets follow the allocation (ruling 15; extends Z03-9).** Every process, research included, loads providers under an allocation; the shares on one provider add up to at most 10,000 bps of each budget, and the gateway meters response bytes against 50% of each documented byte limit times this process's share (`ProviderConfig.budgetShareBps`).
- **Z03-17 · Addresses inside longer strings (ruling 16).** The lint rule checks every run of base58 characters in a string literal, so `solana:<address>`, padded addresses and URL paths are reported outside constants.ts and fixture files. Concatenation and JSON config stay out of scope (Z03-9).
- **Z03-18 · A curve quoted in anything but SOL (ruling 17).** A `pump_bonding_curve` whose `quote_mint` is not `Pubkey::default()` (native SOL) decodes as `unknown`, so its reserves never become Lamports; it is counted as `decode_accounts_total{kind="pump_bonding_curve",result="non_sol_quote"}`. The variant's `quoteMint` is therefore always `null`. Reading `rpc.providers_file` (with its root-owned check) is recorded on the M26 card (ruling 19).
- **Z03-19 · Allocation checks (round 4, rulings 22–24).** A malformed allocation (no `shares`, a non-object `shares` or provider entry, no consumer) is `E_CONFIG`, never a TypeError. In the engine context, fewer than two live read providers with a nonzero share for this consumer (an unknown consumer, `__proto__`, `shares: []` included) is `E_CONFIG` at start: ARCH D04 needs a primary and a backup. A nonzero share that leaves a provider below one request a minute (`MIN_SHARED_RPS`) is `E_CONFIG`; only 0 turns a provider off for a process.
- **Z03-20 · The floor on every rate, and backups that serve P0 reads (round 5, rulings 25–26).** A nonzero share must leave each scaled rate of a provider at one request a minute or more: `rps`, `heavyRps`, `perMethodRps`, `sendRps`, and the part of `rps` below the P0 reserve (`rpc.p0_reserve_bps`, passed to `loadProviders` as `p0ReserveBps`). A provider counts toward the engine's two live readers only if it serves every method in `P0_READ_METHODS` (getAccountInfo, getMultipleAccounts, getTransaction, getSlot, getSignatureStatuses, getLatestBlockhash: the send, confirm and exit reads of ARCH M14's P0).
- **Z03-21 · The floor uses the gateway's own reserve (round 6, rulings 30–31).** `getTokenAccountBalance` joins `P0_READ_METHODS` (the sell amount at exit and the evidence poll, ARCH M19/M20). `RegistryOptions.p0ReserveBps` is required (1,000–5,000, no default), and `createRpcGateway` checks again, with its own `p0ReserveBps`, that every provider loaded under a share keeps one request a minute; it throws a RangeError otherwise. Both checks use one function, `floorProblems`, which also holds the heavy, per-method and send rates to the floor below the P0 reserve, because every bucket of the gateway keeps that reserve (a builder's widening of ruling 25, which named the part of `rps` only).
- **Z03-22 · Fixture files after the Z05 merge (supervisor ruling 32).** The address-literal exemption no longer covers every `packages/*/src/fixtures.ts`: only `fixtures/**`, `packages/*/test/fixtures.ts`, and two named src files, `packages/dashboard/src/fixtures.ts` and `packages/contract/src/fixtures.ts`. The ruling named the dashboard file and test paths; the frozen `@bot/contract/fixtures` (random test keys, B-M28-01, imported by contract tests only) also needs it, so it is named rather than left failing lint (flagged to the supervisor). In those files `bot/no-program-id-literal` still runs, with `allowAddresses`, so a `PROGRAMS` id there fails lint; only the constants registry turns the rule off. A new rule, `bot/no-fixtures-import`, refuses any import, re-export, dynamic import or `require()` of a module named `fixtures` outside the dashboard catalogue and `**/test/**`, so production code cannot take an address from a fixtures file. The red team's repro (the pump id in `packages/engine/src/fixtures.ts`, imported by `packages/engine/src/a.ts`) now fails lint twice.

## Z04 Recorder queue (2026-10-08, supervisor rulings for the round 1 review and red team, `docs/reviews/Z04.md`)

- **Seq is assigned when the writer takes a record, not on `append`** (`packages/engine/src/recorder/queue.ts` `take`). A record dropped in the queue, or a snapshot the writer finds unchanged, never reaches disk; assigning seq at take keeps each stream's seq gap-free over the records written, which A-M07-02's manifests count (`firstSeq..lastSeq`). Order within a stream is still append order, and seq continues after a restart from the last manifest's `lastSeq + 1`.
- **The recorder queue has a 64 MiB byte limit besides `recorder.queue_max`** (`RecorderQueueConfig.queueMaxBytes`). The ticket bounds only the count: 50,000 records at the 64 KB record cap would be about 3.2 GB, more than the 2 GB host (D07) holds, and the 6 Oct restart loop was V8 running out of memory (`docs/MIGRATION.md` "Bugs left behind"). Payload, `source` and a 256-byte per-record allowance are counted.
- **A poll is counted as changed only against a previous hash in the same watch** (Z04 round 4, ruling 18; `packages/engine/src/recorder/queue.ts` `append`). "Changed" needs two hashes: the first poll of a watch, after a restart or after the pool was unwatched, has nothing to compare with, so it counts in `successfulPolls` but not in `changedPolls`. Counting it as a change would report changes that were never seen, each time a pool comes back. Hence an hour of 40 states counts 39 changes; the snapshot written for the first state is still a keyframe.
- **A silent feed's `poll_counts` arrive as one combined record when it resumes** (Z04 round 7, ruling 29; `packages/engine/src/recorder/queue.ts` `tick`). The recorder never writes a minute past the newest minute it received data for + 1 (ruling 25), so while no producer record arrives, `poll_counts` stop. When the feed resumes, the silent minutes are written: one by one when there are at most `pollCatchUpMaxMinutes` (10) of them, else as one record per pool with `skippedMinutes` and the summed counts (all zero for a silent feed). A replay reads those minutes as not observed. With the writer's tick clock steady and no record for more than `outageMinutes` (5), the episode is logged once as `M07.outage` and counted in `outages`, apart from clock steps, which are logged once per episode as `M07.clock_step`.
- **The recorder's gaps, and how A-M07-03 reads them** (Z04 rounds 8 and 9, rulings 33, 39 and 40; `packages/engine/src/recorder/queue.ts` `Gap`, `drainGaps`). A gap with a `poolId` excludes only that pool's minutes from coverage, and counts toward the 5% `low_coverage` rule (ARCH M07) as that pool's share. A gap with no `poolId` (any `backpressure` gap, or poll gaps of two pools merged past `GAPS_MAX_PER_STREAM`) excludes the whole stream for its span. On `poll_counts` the per-pool reasons are `clock_step` (a poll a clock step moved more than one minute) and `moved` (a poll simply that late, no step); each marks the pool's minute the poll was stamped in and the minute it was counted in. A poll moved by one minute gets no gap: its record carries `latePolls` and `lateFromMinuteStartMs`, and A-M07-03 credits that earlier minute as observed. `Gap` is local to `@bot/engine`; the frozen `@bot/types` `CoverageReport.gaps` keeps `{ fromMs, toMs, reason }`, and A-M07-03 maps these gaps into it.

## PATHS-FIX round 2 (2026-10-09, supervisor rulings 19–23, `docs/reviews/DISKBUDGET.md` @ b4164fbe)

- **The receipts sweep never recurses** (ruling 19). Files and links go with `unlink`, folders only with `rmdir`, anything else is reported as stuck. A swap between the check and the delete can only fail; it never reaches outside `receipts/`.
- **`receipts/` is its own filesystem** (ruling 20). It is a root-only 64 MiB ext4 image with 1 KiB blocks and 32,768 inodes, loop-mounted at `/var/lib/zeroed-md/receipts` by `zeroed-receipts-fs.service`.
  - The image is preallocated (ruling 26): `fallocate`, and `mkfs.ext4 -E nodiscard,lazy_itable_init=0,lazy_journal_init=0`. A default mkfs discards and frees the blocks again (measured: 131,072 to 8,960 512-byte blocks). With lazy init, the kernel zeroes the inode tables after the first mount through the loop device, which punches holes in the image (measured in the ops e2e host image: 131,080 to 114,768 within 20 s; the ops e2e caught it). So it takes exactly 64 MiB of the server's disk, counted in the disk budget, and can never fail later for lack of space.
  - Measured: 32,757 receipts fit, then every write fails with ENOSPC. That fails closed: a segment with no receipt is only kept longer.
  - At the recorder's 30-day retention this is room for about 45 streams an hour. The stream count is UNVERIFIED.
  - A receipt's file name is bound to its segment (sha256 of the segment path), so there is one receipt per segment. A copy under any other name is deleted.
- **Who writes `rpc-usage.db`** (ruling 21). SPEC-A A-M14 (the account ledger) says every consumer checks and writes it before each send, with owner `bot`, group `sentinel` and mode 0660. Its allocation lists the engine (worker and sentinel) as one consumer and the signer's block as separate: the signer never writes the ledger. So the sentinel writes it too, and it cannot sit in the engine's 0700 state folder.
  - It moves to `/var/lib/zeroed-usage/rpc-usage.db`. The folder is made by the installer: owner `zeroed-worker`, group `zeroed-sentinel` (the spec's `sentinel` group on this host), mode 2770 setgid. The engine unit lists it in `ReadWritePaths`.
  - M14 sets mode 0660 itself on the database and its `-wal`/`-shm` files, because the unit's `UMask=0077` would otherwise make them 0600 and shut the sentinel out.
  - In the backup (ruling 24). `zeroed-backup` also copies `/var/lib/zeroed-usage` with SQLite's online backup, as it does the state folder's databases. The copy is stored under `zeroed-usage/` in the same encrypted bundle, so it is counted in the same per-copy size and in the off-server copy's 50 MB limit. `zeroed-restore-drill` checks it against the live ledger. A restore without it would undercount the 31-day sums.
  - Card notes (ruling 25, no code now):
    - The sentinel's user and unit (B-M30-02) do not exist on the host yet. Its unit must run with group `zeroed-sentinel`.
    - M14 must create the database and its `-wal` and `-shm` files as 0660, because the engine unit's `UMask=0077` would otherwise make them 0600.
    - The sentinel opens `rpc-usage.db` and its `-wal` and `-shm` files only after an `lstat` check that each is a regular file, or with `O_NOFOLLOW` (ruling 27). The folder is group-writable, so a link planted there must never lead it to another file.
    - B-M30-02 makes the backup of `/var/lib/zeroed-usage` run as `zeroed-worker`, or only after an `lstat` check that each file is a regular file (ruling 33). `zeroed-backup` runs as root, and the folder is writable by the sentinel's group.
    - M14 makes the engine itself `lstat`-check `rpc-usage.db` and its `-wal` and `-shm` files before opening them, or open them with `O_NOFOLLOW` (ruling 33).
- **The worker starts after the receipts filesystem** (ruling 32). `zeroed-worker.service` has `After=` and `Wants=zeroed-receipts-fs.service`, so the worker never sees `receipts/` before the filesystem is mounted. A failed mount does not keep the worker down; `zeroed-check` alerts on it.
- **SPEC fixes** (rulings 21–22). SPEC-A:1307 (day index path), SPEC-A:2513 (usage ledger), SPEC-B:2031 (no M24 backup step; `zeroed-backup` is the one owner) and SPEC-B:2584 (`/var/lib/zeroed` replaces `/var/lib/bot`; the usage folder added).
- **Pull mounts are watched** (ruling 23). `zeroed-check` raises one alert while the receipts filesystem or either chroot bind is not active, and a CLEARED line after.

## IDL-REPIN pin 8cda1fa (2026-10-08, builder decisions for review)

Card IDL-REPIN (`docs/reviews/Z03.md` "IDL-REPIN"; findings in `docs/reviews/VERIFYNEXT.md` V22) re-pins the vendored IDLs to pump-public-docs `8cda1fa30ea658b20909d8aedf002047119388d2`, the repository head when read on 2026-10-08, with the sha256 values of V22. The supervisor's rulings carried in: `multi_hop_swap` trades stay dropped under `unpinned_invoker` until a real multi-hop fixture proves the hop check; `pump_post_complete_buy` stays in `@bot/decoders`, with no `@bot/types` bump. The owner approved running the tests with these IDL files on 2026-10-08.

- **IDL-REPIN-1 · Short legacy accounts beyond the curve.** The new pin appends fields to `BondingCurve` (`creator_fee` to `post_complete_quote_in`), pump `Global` (`max_curve_depth`) and `Pool` (`protocol_fees`, `creator_fees`). The recorded pump `Global` is 1 byte short of the new layout and recorded curves end inside it (143 bytes after the discriminator), so without padding they would decode as `unknown`. From the last field each type had at `cb188ce`, an account is read with its missing fields as 0 (`shortLegacy`) when it ends on a field boundary, or when its bytes past the last boundary are all zero (allocated capacity under the old pin, never data). Below that field, the C03-R1-4 rule is unchanged: curves only on a boundary; `Global` and `Pool` stay `unknown`. Sources: pump-public-docs `8cda1fa` `PUMP_PROGRAM_README.md` ("Fields appended to `BondingCurve`"), `PUMP_SWAP_README.md` ("Pools written before an appended field existed"), `instructions/SWEEP_FEES.md` ("Account layout"), and `extend_account` for `Global` ("to allow adding new fields"). Ruling 34, as built: in an account that ends past the old end but not on a field boundary, every byte of the partial field must be zero, or the account is `unknown`; a test sets each byte of each partial length. Whole new fields past the old end are read as written: the recorded C11 curves `586AJyoo` and `BRe3zx83` carry non-zero `creator_fee` (7,683,875 and 1,119,054 lamports) and `protocol_fees` there, so "every byte past the old end is zero" would make current curves `unknown` (raised with the supervisor).
- **IDL-REPIN-2 · Short legacy events.** `TradeEvent`, `BuyEvent` and `SellEvent` gain `creator_fee_unclaimed`, and `CreateEvent` gains `depth`; every other field of every event is unchanged (checked against both IDLs). Without padding, every trade emitted between the holder-rewards layout and 2026-10-02 would be `truncated`. Events from before the holder-rewards layout (pump-public-docs dated 2026-09-12; the mainnet date is UNVERIFIED) are shorter than the `cb188ce` floor and stay `truncated` (ruling 36): counted, never decoded. The B-10 days (2026-07-23 to 08-21) may be in such older layouts, so Z-H and PM01-KILL need the IDLs in force on those days before they rely on this decoder (`docs/reviews/Z03.md`, IDL-REPIN #313, supervisor note on MINOR 5). An event whose data ends on a field boundary at or after its `cb188ce` last field reads the rest as 0, flagged `shortLegacy` on `LocatedEvent`; data ending inside a field is still `truncated`. Source: `HOLDER_REWARDS_README.md` ("Logs emitted before the new fields existed are shorter; read the missing fields as `0` / `false`"). Consumer rule (ruling 35): code that reads a field the pin appended (`creator_fee_unclaimed`, `depth`, and the appended account fields of IDL-REPIN-1) checks `shortLegacy` first, because 0 there may mean "not yet in the layout", not a real zero; a guard test pins the flag to exactly the padded cases.
- **IDL-REPIN-3 · `unpinned_invoker`.** A PumpSwap trade whose invoking instruction is not in the pinned IDL, or whose invoker data is too short or too long to be one, is refused as `unpinned_invoker`; before, it was `non_sol_quote`. A pinned instruction that binds no quote mint (`create_pool`) stays `non_sol_quote`, as ruling 14 set.
- **IDL-REPIN-4 · `PostCompleteBuyEvent`.** It decodes as `pump_post_complete_buy`: `quote_in` as `solAmount`, `base_out` as `tokenAmount`, with the fee and creator fee and their bps; it is refused as `non_sol_quote` unless its `quote_mint` is `Pubkey::default()`. `pumpBuyTotals` sums `pump_trade` buys and `pump_post_complete_buy` per signature, top-level instruction and coin. `decodeEvents` and `decodeTransactionEvents` keep to `DecodedEvent` and leave it out; `decode_events_total{kind="pump_post_complete_buy"}` counts it. Round 2: `onGapAt` reports each gap with its place and IDL event name (`LocatedGap`), and `pumpBuyTotals(events, gaps)` marks a total `incomplete` when a TradeEvent, a PostCompleteBuyEvent or an unnamed event gapped in its top-level instruction (ruling 37). Whether a SOL curve's PostCompleteBuyEvent carries the default key or wSOL as `quote_mint` is UNVERIFIED; a golden from a real mainnet completing v3 buy is required before any card reads the totals (Z08, PM01-KILL). `LocatedEvent.multiHop` marks an event with a PumpSwap `multi_hop_swap` among its invokers (top-level or inner); such pump events still decode but stay out of the totals until the multi-hop fixture lands (ruling 38). Round 3: `decodeEventsWithGaps` (and `Decoders.decodeTransactionEventsWithGaps`) returns `{events, gaps}` from one decode, and `pumpBuyTotals` takes that result whole (rulings 43, 46), so no caller gets a total that reads complete for lack of its gaps. An invoker chain that cannot be followed, and pump `multi_hop_curve_swap` as an invoker, also count as a route (ruling 44; `PinnedIdl.routeInstructions`), decided by the 8-byte discriminator whatever the data length (ruling 45).
- **IDL-REPIN-5 · Sweeps bind the pool too.** At `8cda1fa` the set built from `relations: ["pool"]` also holds `sweep_creator_fee` and `sweep_protocol_fee`, with `quote_mint` at account 3. They emit `SweepPoolFeeEvent`, which maps to no `DecodedEvent`, so their membership changes no output; the rule stays "built from the IDL", not a hand list.
- **IDL-REPIN-6 · Constants re-derived.** The seeds of the four PDAs in `@bot/venue/constants` and of the pool-authority PDA are the ones the `8cda1fa` IDLs give (`global`, `global_config`, `fee_config` with each program, `pool-authority` with the base mint under pump). A new venue test compares them with the pinned IDL files, so a later pin that changes a seed fails.
- **IDL-REPIN-7 · DEC-1 goldens.** At the new pin no decoded DEC-1 value changes. 22 Z03-vocabulary values in `fixtures/dec1/goldens.json` do: 7 account flags, 14 events' `layoutExtended` (the 8 bytes are now `creator_fee_unclaimed`), and pump `Global`'s new `max_curve_depth` 0. The five non-SOL trades are still refused. They were applied from a full diff of decoder output against the goldens, which found no other difference.
